Summary

Vidal Corp. was an ambitious company, known for its revolutionary work ethic, zero to none layoffs and extraordinary pay. While the ex- almost-CEO did indeed mostly look forward to the financial argument, she knew she had to take this opportunity as soon as possible and secure her place before anyone else even wasted a thought in doing so. The company would have no other choice but to hire her. Because Agatha Harkness would not miss up a chance to present Evanora Harkness the biggest middlefinger known to mankind, now that her very own daughter would be applying her expertise in Harkness Corps biggest rival company.

or A year after getting fired from her own family business, Agatha Harkness starts an accounting job at Vidal Corp. In her attempt to work herself from the bottom back to the top, she is met with several unforeseen obstacles, including an unplanned rush of feelings for her new boss, Rio Vidal.

    Chapter 1: Chapter One

    Chapter Notes Hello there!

    It has been ages since I've last published a fanfiction anywhere on the internet. Agatha All Along still has me in a massive chokehold and urged me to write this.

    I am not a native english speaker, so there are definitely going to be a few mistakes along the way. Sorry for that, please don't kill me as I haven't finished my bachelor's degree yet.

    Feel free to point them out and I shall fix them asap.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes When the elevator doors finally opened after what felt like an eternity, Agatha Harkness forced her best smile onto her lips. If anyone had told her a few years ago that she was about to step foot into her rival's office as anything else than a CEO trying to completely fuck this place up, she would've probably choked to death on her own laughter. But there was certainly no reason for her to laugh now – except for trying to leave a good impression on her new co-workers (although she did not really give a shit about them, anyways).

    It has been an entire year ever since her own mother has thrown her out of the family business. After years and years of hard work, literally dedicating her entire life to her work, Agatha was sure she would finally be promoted as the rightful CEO of Harkness Corp., but her mother did not think her worthy enough. And if that wasn't enough already, Agatha did not only lose her job, but her general work profits as well. No car, no apartment. The life she was used to, gone in an instant.

    There was no way around a fresh start. Agatha hated fresh starts. Thankfully though, Wanda, an old friend from highschool, was there to catch her. After staying at her place for a while, Agatha managed to snatch a decent apartment in a city nearby. Job hunting took its toll on her, chasing her through various departments, all of them more than unworthy of the Agatha Harkness, renowned business woman of one of the world's highest grossing industries. None of said jobs lasted long, and there seemed to finally be a light at the end of the tunnel when one of Wanda's sons had informed her about the internship he was about to take on. The same company was said to be hiring for an accountant, which immediately piqued Agatha's interest.

    Vidal Corp. was an ambiguous company, known for its revolutionary work ethic, zero to none layoffs and extraordinary pay. While the ex-almost-CEO did indeed mostly look forward to the financial argument, she knew she had to take this opportunity as soon as possible and secure her place before anyone else even wasted a thought in doing so. The company would have no other choice but to hire her. Because Agatha Harkness would not miss up a chance to present Evanora Harkness the biggest middlefinger known to mankind, now that her very own daughter would be applying her expertise in Harkness Corps.' biggest rival company.

    The applying process has been dreadful already. One last conversation and she would finally be able to secure her job and get back to business. Well, not entirely the business Agatha was hoping for, but she knew the ways of company ethics and strategies on how to hurdle herself to the top. She has done it once, and she will do it again. But this time, she will secure her success once and for all, regardless of what her mother thought of her talents.

    "Good morning, Miss-" a blonde lady greeted her.

    "Harkness. Agatha Harkness."

    She reached out her hand, shaking the woman's, all the while assuming that she must be the secretary of her new boss.

    New boss. The thought of it almost made Agatha gag.

    "My name is Adeline Reed. I'm Ms. Vidal's personal secretary." The brunette couldn't help but slightly raise her eyebrow at the emphasis on the word 'personal'.

    "I believe we have spoken on the phone before, have we not?" "Yes, we have. You were the one walking me through the application process."

    Agatha gripped the documents in her hands a little tighter. As if she were oblivious to how this process worked. She's been a part of it countless of times already – on the other end of the stick specifically, much more to the older woman's liking, instead of ... whatever this is supposed to be. "Well then, this is going to be your final conversation. Ms. Vidal is going to have a personal talk with you, evaluate your expertise, test you in..." Agatha wished nothing more but to turn this woman off. She kept on holding her gaze but didn't mind her words any further attention. Analyzing the features of the secretary before her, Agatha figured that she could be no older than 30. Bright blue eyes, blonde hair and a face adorned with dimples. Something in the back of Agatha's mind told her they weren't genuine ones.

    "Ms. Vidal will be seeing you now," Adeline ensured, snapping Agatha back into existence.

    Nodding her head quickly, she swiftly made her way into the office. As the door closed behind her, she took in the interior of the room. Nice and tidy, no personal items in sight. It made her think of the office she used to have.

    When she dropped her gaze to the person sitting at a wooden desk, she was met with a pair of gorgeous brown eyes. The woman in front of her smiled faintly, placing her pen besides the document she was going through just a moment ago. She was dressed in a formidable dark grey suit, her white shirt not fully buttoned up. Her brown hair was held up by a large clip, making all of her facial features perfectly visible.

    Agatha made her way towards the desk and stretched out her hand. "Agatha Harkness, my pleasure," she said, not once breaking their shared eye contact. The CEO took the offered hand and her smile grew, showing the edges of her canines.

    "Rio Vidal, pleased to finally make your acquaintance."

    The handshake was firm and Agatha couldn't help but notice how soft Rio's skin was.

    "You know, I have heard a lot about you over the years, Agatha Harkness. I never thought you would eventually resurface back on my end," Rio said, shoving the document aside that was still occupying part of the table. Agatha snorted lightly.

    "What can I say? I'm full of surprises," she said, purposefully letting a tint of sarcasm break through, earning a smirk from the other woman. Straightening her position in the chair, Agatha started placing her documents in front of her.

    "I assume you have read most articles about me already?" she asked, nudging her head slightly to the side. The press was going wild on the fateful day of her dismissal and only ever added to all the other articles culminating several moments of Agatha's previous worklife. It was easy enough to catch up on her reputation with a simple Google Search. "Oh, I sure did," Rio answered. "But those don't really matter right now. You're looking for a new job. I'm here to evaluate."

    A small pause.

    "I assume those are all of your application documents?"

    "Yes."

    In a swift moment, the papers left Agatha's hands to wander to the CEO in front of her.

    "I have been working as an accountant at my family business for many years until I was tasked with other necessities. I basically grew up with it and could recite the basics in my sleep," she explained, earning a nod in return. There is not going to be a single person who could fulfill this role better than me."

    The older woman curved her chin upwards, her voice filled with unbreakable certainty.

    "Ah, is that so?" Rio gazed up from the documents, raising an eyebrow. "I got a whole line of interviews after this one, all of them applying for the same job as you do."

    Agatha squeezed her eyes slightly shut. She knew the schemes Rio was using and was more than ready to simply feed into them. She knew that she was testing her.

    "They can pack up and go back home. I have more years in experience than they have in walking this earth."

    Agatha knew this wasn't the most formal way to interact with your future boss. But she knew the field, she knew what most of them always oh so desire to hear. It was never anything but the total reassurance that the company would get the best there is. And Agatha surely was exactly that. "My work contributed to an impeccable boost of Harkness Corps' financial profits. We were able to up our revenue by 20 percent within the mere span of a single year back in 2016."

    "I do not doubt your talent, Miss Harkness. It would surely be a waste as to not consider it for this institution," Rio admitted, placing the documents back on the table. Agatha almost secured this as a guaranteed win, but knew (by all means, unfortunately all too well now) that pride always comes before the fall.

    The younger woman tilted her head to the side and crossed her arms. "Tell me why exactly you chose Vidal Corp. as your future workplace. There's many fish in the sea, why choose to hunt after this one, specifically?"

    So I can contribute to your company with my skills. Because I am the best and you won't find a better person than me. Because I love your work ethic and dream of being a part of your team. Every single one of them was merely beating a dead horse. They've been ingrained into Agatha's skull by hundreds of peers who have tried to slither their way into her former company just a few years ago.

    Agatha smirked and leaned forward.

    "I would greatly contribute to the success of this company, because I am the best at my craft."

    After a few seconds of silence, Rio receeded to her previous position, placing her hands back on the desk.

    "Why are you really here, Agatha?", she asked again, her voice more tender than before.

    The sole way Rio mentioned her name this time sent a slight shiver down Agatha's spine. She forced yet another smile.

    "That, I have already answered. I meant what I said," she replied drily. Rio pushed her tongue against the inside of her cheek. "I don't buy that." Agatha held her gaze for a while, her smirk only growing wider. Rio knew she wasn't entirely honest regarding her reason of being here. But she also knew about Agatha's skillset. Although they've never met in person before, Agatha has been fed numerous information thanks to press and an egregious amount of gossip. Past conferences gave a little more insight into the opposing company and its owners. She has had a picture of Rio in the back of her mind for a while now. She believed Rio must've had one of Agatha as well.

    The CEO shifted in her seat yet again and started culminating Agatha's documents into their original state. She got back on her feet and extended her arm towards the older woman.

    "Welcome to Vidal Corp.. I am looking forward to working with you, Agatha Harkness"

    After another firm handshake, Rio led Agatha back to the door of the office. "My assistant will be showing you around the building. I hope you settle in fast", her new boss explained.

    Agatha simply nodded, reveling in the first real success in what felt like half a century. "Oh, I sure will", she said.

    Rio gave her a smile and opened the door.

    "Te veo", she whispered, her eyes lingering on Agatha until she made her way out of Rio's office.

    Simply cooking to a burnt chip in hell seemed like a better deal than following Ms. Vidal's personal secretary around the industry complex like an obedient chihuaha. "God fucking damn it", Agatha mumbled under her breath after finally getting led out of the cafeteria.

    "Sorry, what did you say?", Adeline inquired.

    "Oh, just a little overwhelmed," the woman replied with the nicest possible tone she could muster up in the current situation.

    After wasting enough time on looking at rooms she would never return to and greeting people she would never see again in probably the entire course of her career, Agatha finally made her way to her cubicle. Dropping her documents carelessly on the table, she exhaled with an annoyed tone. Her boss' personal pet was definitely too enthusiastic and energetic to her liking. "Agatha!"

    A head suddenly popped up from beyond her cubicle walls.

    "So, you got the job?"

    "I find your lack of faith in me disturbing, Billy," she replied in her usual snarky tone.

    The young man made his way towards Agatha's desk.

    "I can't believe we're going to be cubiclemates," he said enthusiastically. Agatha furrowed her eyebrows. She still could not hold up with Billy's weird slang. "That's not a word"

    "Agatha fucking Harkness?"

    Another head popped up close to Agatha's personal working space. A furious face met that of the newly hired woman.

    "Jen!" Agatha exclaimed in a surprised tone, morphing her mouth into a big smile. "Fancy seeing you here. How's the YouTube channel going?" Jennifer raised an eyebrow. "How's the family business going?", she snapped back.

    Agatha let herself fall to her chair with an audible 'tsk'. "Wait, you guys already know each other?" Billy asked curiously, totally oblivious to the tension in the room.

    "How could I ever forget the person who literally got me fired from my last job?" Jen snarled and continued to stare daggers into Agatha's skin. "Whoa there, that was a looong time ago," she started out, waving her hand in an attempt to brush this past maneuver to the side. "Besides, I am sure you thank me for that by now. Considering the state the company is in right now – eugh, I really did save your ass, Jen."

    The woman, still standing within her own cubicle, shook her head with a grin. "Don't expect me to fall to my knees in front of you, Harkness." Agatha's eyes grew wide in a theatrical fashion.

    "Jen, not in front of the kid", she gasped dramatically.

    Billy furrowed his eyebrows in confusion.

    "Guys, lets's just chill out for a minute. We're going to be working together from now on!" He clearly tried to ease the tension that was ever present within the office room.

    "Yay!" Agatha balled her hands into a pair of fists, wobbled them in front of her chest in an excited motion and threw a wide grin towards Kale. "Yippee," Jen breathed, slowly retreating back to her cubicle. Once Billy was seated at his own desk again, Agatha was ready to dive into her first task of the day.

    "You never told me you had a YouTube channel, Jen," Billy said. "I don't. Get back to work, Billy," Jen replied sharply.

    The brunette shook her head and buried her hands in her strands. An emo boy who has been annoying her ever since he took his first breath and an ex-colleague-now-colleague-again who was still holding a giant grudge against her for getting her fired years ago. Add an overly enthusiastic and slightly obsessive secretary to the list, and finally, top it off with her former rival in the economic field who seemed a little too eager to welcome her in the company.

    Agatha was pissed. She was supposed to be sitting in her own office, in her own company, leading her own team instead of being led. All of what has been taken from her was a constant reminder of her wasted efforts. And now that another chance had arisen, she would do anything to get what is truly hers and make those efforts worth it.

    Agatha Harkness gotta get back on top. And she will.

    Chapter End Notes Thanks for reading this mess, it really means a lot!

    Since this is going to be more of a slow burn, it will take a little while for them to warm up to each other. But I can for sure tell you that Agatha will indeed get on top eventually ... in some sorta sense. I'll try to update as soon as possible.

    Have a good one!

    Chapter 2: Chapter Two

    Chapter Notes Hello there!

    Sorry for the wait. Uni is keeping me pretty busy at the moment, but I'll try to update as frequently as possible.

    First of all, I want to thank each and every person who stumbled upon this fic and decided to give it a read. Thank you so much for the lovely comments on the first chapter, for the kudos and just for reading in general. Thank you for the support, it means the world to me :) I updated the first chapter and changed the formatting a little. I also fixed some spelling errors as well.

    This chapter was originally planned to be longer, but I ultimately decided to split it into two and rework it into separate chapters. The pacing felt off and I didn't want to put too much on the table just yet.

    Chapter 3 is already done as well, I'll fix some minor things and it

    should be up in a few days.

    I also wanted to note that despite being taught some stuff about accounting a few years prior, I have no idea how all of this works actually. I looked some stuff up in order to make it a little more understandable but if I'm being honest, I have no idea if what I write about even makes sense. I feel like I'm just making stuff up at this point. Hope that's okay, as the contents of the accounting work/corporate stuff is not the main core of this story. Gotta focus on the gays.

    Sorry for the long note. Enjoy!

    See the end of the chapter for more notes "How's work going?" Wanda chimed through the phone, eager to know about her best friend's new chapter in life. It has taken the redhead some time to reach her, eventually managing to catch her on the way to work. "A little more underwhelming than I initially anticipated," Agatha pressed the phone closer to her ear, now that a few more people had entered the train. No car meant to commute via public transportation, something the woman has always rather despised.

    "I'm sure you'll get used to it. Give it some time."

    "I have to, one way or another. They wouldn't want to promote someone who refuses to fit in," she mumbled.

    "You've been employed at this company for less than two weeks and you're already thinking about promotion? Agatha, slow down a minute and just ... enjoy your new life," the redhead said. Agatha rolled her eyes. Wanda was talking to her the very same way she did to Billy. She definitely is no child no more.

    "I'm enjoying my life just fine, thank you."

    "Your life shouldn't solely revolve around your job, you know. Have some fun, get out, meet new people."

    The train announced its next stop, blending over whatever Wanda continued to say after. Agatha could only muster that it'd be yet another pep talk about how important it is to live in the moment and take pleasure in the other aspects of life apart from work.

    The doors of the train opened in a staggering sequence and dozens of people were ready to head towards their destination. Agatha switched her phone to the other ear as she exited the vehicle.

    "Soooo ..."

    Oh no. She knew that pitch in Wanda's voice all to well by now. "About Miss Vidal ..."

    "What about her?" the brunette inquired almost a little too eager. "What do you think about her?"

    Wanda was literally distances away from her friend, but Agatha knew exactly that she was wearing a big grin on her face while throwing that question in.

    "Well, I haven't really seen much of her, but I can certainly tell that she's good at her job. A little more laid back than I imagined, but she gets her shit done."

    "Apart from work, Agatha. What is she like, as a person, to you?" "She's nice, I guess."

    She made her way further towards the building of Vidal Corp. "She's also totally your type," Wanda smirked.

    "Wanda, come on, that's not true."

    "Ohh, Agatha, I've known you for, what, over 25 years now? Judging from all the girls that had your tongue down their throat, I can definitely tell that she is your type."

    Wanda tried to recall the moment she first laid eyes upon Rio herself. She remembered sitting in her office together with Billy to talk about the internship he applied for. The woman almost perfectly hit every criteria of Agatha's type and Wanda knew exactly that she could tease her friend with that knowledge.

    A pause. All the redhead could hear was the noise of the traffic and Agatha's staggered breath caused by a quicker walking pace.

    "Fine. Yes, she is an attractive woman. Maybe even my type. But she is also my boss, Wanda."

    "And that would be a problem, cause ... ?"

    "You know exactly why I applied for this job. Stuff like this would only complicate things even further."

    Agatha could almost feel the amused shake of her friend's head. "Come on, Agatha. Live a little."

    "I for sure am going to live in severe agony if I keep on talking to you." She made her way into the building and started hurrying towards the elevator. As she clicked the button that would transport her straight to the cubicles, she realized that Wanda was still talking.

    "Who knows, maybe you're her type as well? Billy said he's pretty sure that she's single-"

    "Toodles!"

    Agatha removed the phone from her ear.

    She almost bore her finger right through her phone screen in order to hit the hang up button. Silence fell upon her like a warm hug and she took a moment to bathe in that feeling.

    It all left just as qickly as it came when a sudden ting disturbed the moment. Agatha looked back on her phone, her face immediately painted with anger. 1 New Message from birth giver.

    This fucking hag was the last thing she wanted to hear anything about today (or, ever). Agatha simply swiped her mother's message away, not bothering what she would be ranting about this time. Her mother hasn't messaged or called her for an entire year. She could wait another few days for an answer. And that was already too generous according to Agatha's tastes. Leaving the elevator with a loud sigh, she made her way straight to her work cubicle. She could hear Billy going through some finances with Jennifer's help. Agatha put her bag on the desk, scrambled all of her papers together and got to work.

    After about an hour, an additional voice filled the void around them. Rio joined both Billy and Jen in their cubicle, gathering information about how Billy was doing so far, now that he also took part in some accounting duties for the time being. Agatha tried to blend out their voices, focusing on the task at hand. A few minutes passed and she felt a figure slowly approach her from the back.

    "Buenos días, Ms. Harkness," Rio's soft voice caught her attention. She turned in her seat, gazing upon the woman who was now standing a few inches apart from her, hands in the pockets of her trousers. "Morning, Ms. Vidal," she replied, fingers leaving the keyboard to abandon her current Excel table.

    "How's it going?"

    Agatha shifted her body more towards her boss. "It's going pretty well. The reports should be in by-"

    "Sorry, that's not what I meant. How is it going, as in, how are you settling in?" she asked again, nudging her head to the side, letting her brown hair cascade over her shoulder. Agatha dropped her gaze for a moment, following the strands and noticing how they really accentuated Rio's uniform today.

    "Uh-"

    Agatha regained her posture, locking eyes with her boss.

    "Good. Feels just like home;" she said, gesturing around her empty cubicle. Rio nodded and followed her hand movements.

    "Feel free to decorate your place, if you want. There are no restrictions, so if you ever feel like putting up some posters or family pictures ..." "And have my mother stare down at me 24/7? She's been doing that for 45 years already, it's getting boring," Agatha let out an awkward chuckle and started fidgeting with her keyboard.

    Rio reacted with a stifled laugh. She swiftly looked down and bit her bottom lip before glancing back at Agatha.

    "I get what you mean. I'm also rather opposed to have my parents stare me down while I sign another batch of reports."

    Agatha couldn't help but let out a quick laugh as well. She did not know anything about Rio's relationship with her family, no public article has ever mentioned anything in that regard. Deep down she knew that they had a shared sentiment of some sorts. It gave Agatha a weird feeling of comfort. "Look, whatever you decide to do with your cubicle, I'm sure it'll look great." Rio straightened her back, gifting Agatha another sweet smile. "I have to head back to my office now. If you need anything, don't hesitate to ask. You know where to find me", she assured her.

    Agatha simply nodded without a word.

    "Te veo."

    The CEO made her way out of the cubicle and disappeared in the hallway. Agatha slowly turned back to her computer. Te veo. Rio seems to really like that phrase, she mustered. Eventually, she focused all of her attention back on the Excel table that was still waiting to be completed.

    "This is awful," Agatha moaned.

    She was on the fifth paper of a document that was nothing more but an entire mess. Said document was left from whoever worked in her position beforehand, leaving Agatha with more questions than answers. "Who- Who wrote this shit?"

    "Agatha, can you shut up for once?"

    It wasn't the first time Jen has reminded the brunette to calm the hell down. Billy started to get annoyed by it as well and made his way to Agatha's desk.

    "What's the problem?" he asked, wanting to help her out. Agatha tried to shush him away.

    "This is too advanced for you, kid."

    With an audible groan, Jennifer also made her way to the neighbouring cubicle.

    "Let me", she said and joined Billy in his stance directly behind Agatha, staring over her shoulder to get a look at the god forsaken document. They started bickering about what the problem was, overwhelming Agatha's brain with too much input at once. She was about to explode. "Okay, shut up!" she exclaimed, earning an immediate shhh from a cubicle a little further down the room. All three of them lowered their voices in an instant.

    "The way this document is structured makes me feel sick. You'd have to put the revenue numbers from this date way past these."

    Agatha pointed at the tables on the papers. Both Billy and Jen tried to follow her movements and argumentation, but ultimately just blanked out. "It makes the entire end result much more comprehensive. There also seems to be a calculating mistake in this table following-"

    She accidentaly raised her voice again, immediately conjuring an even louder SHHH than before.

    Jen backed away slowly.

    "Thought this was your expertise, Harkness? Forgot how it works?" she teased in a more or less condescending tone.

    Agatha glared at Jen. She might've become a little rusty over time, but she hasn't had any problems with her daily accounting tasks or assigned reports up until now. This document was horrible enough to cause a tiny crack within Agatha's workflow.

    Billy decided to chime in before the two women could start yet another fight.

    "You'd have to restructure the entire document, wouldn't you?" Agatha sighed deeply.

    "The usual way you're doing it here really won't solve the problem. There is a serious calculation mistake in here. Structuring it in Vidal ways would not only waste too much time, but also turn out rather unplausible," Agatha replied.

    "Why don't you just arrange it the way you're used to?"

    Jennifer dismissed him with a small movement of her hand. "We have our own corporate standards here, Billy. She can't just change the way we're working on things."

    She glanced at Agatha, who was nothing more but a fuming mess. "Guess you'd have to ask the boss for permission," she declared. A grin creeped up the woman's lips, knowing exactly that Agatha would be too proud to be asking someone else for anything even remotely close to permission. Oh, she was enjoying this feeling a little too much to Agatha's liking.

    She put her head in her hands and let her mind wander for a hot second. She hasn't even had her lunch break and Agatha was already close to combusting.

    Wanda trying to play wingman. Her bitch of a mother texting her out of nowhere after one entire year of staying silent. A document that was crafted by the devil himself and now the hitting realisation of having to ask permission from someone else. Maybe she should start following Wanda's advice to live a little and live out these criminal fantasies she was coming up with in the very moment.

    Returning back to her senses, she stared down at her documents. The voices behind her became more quiet.

    "She's not gonna do it herself," Jen whispered.

    Billy shook his head and stood his ground.

    "She is."

    Jen retreated back to her workspace. "Wanna bet that she doesn't?" Agatha sat still for a few minutes. Billy was still standing behind her, briefly looking over the document another time. He decided to retreat to his cubicle after some time.

    Agatha decided it would be best to head to the head of the accounting department first. She grabbed her document and made her way through the room towards said person's workspace.

    After an incredulously unsuccessful discussion with Mr. Proctor, Agatha headed back to her cubicle.

    "Sorry Harkness, can't do anything about that. You gotta ask Ms. Vidal about that"

    His words still rang in her mind. Of course she got to ask the big boss now, of all people.

    Fine.

    Just go ahead and ask for permission.

    It's like ripping off a bandaid, it's gonna be over before you know it. A few meters before Rio's office, Agatha recognized the figure of the secretary standing in front of the door like a guarding dog. "Fuck, no," she muttered under her breath. She had no patience to deal with this woman right now.

    Of course, said woman already waved her hand in dismissal. "Do you have an appointment with Ms. Vidal?" she asked, her eyebrows raising incredibly high, causing her forehead to be covered in wrinkles. She took a look into her small notebook, searching for Agatha's supposed arrival.

    Agatha made no effort to hide her irritation.

    "Appointment? I work here. I need to talk to her, now. It's urgent," she declared.

    She tried to make her way past the blonde, but she was defending her position in front of Rio's door.

    "You can come back later. She's about to have a meeting soon." Her mimic was stern and rather condescending. Agatha was surprised she wasn't met with that facade way earlier. She had a feeling the younger woman did in fact not like her. What a surprise.

    "Look, Ms. Weed-" Agatha started out, immediately earning a shake of the secretary's head in return.

    "It's Ms. Reed," she corrected her.

    "Ms. Reed."

    Agatha couldn't care less.

    "My boss, Ms. Vidal, told me that whenever I needed something, I could just go ahead and ask her. I have a question right now. I will ask her, in this very moment. It won't even take a goddamn minute."

    She made sure the entire thread of sentences was worded in the slowest, most accentuated way so that even a toddler could understand her at this point.

    "Oh, of course she did," the blonde replied in a sarcastic tone. She wasn't believing her.

    The brunette tried pushing her way through and Adeline wasn't having it. The girl tried to defend her stance with all of her might. "You can't just-"

    They were shoving each other around the tiny space occupying the front of the office door.

    "It's urgent," Agatha threw in.

    "Come back once you have an appointment. You have to make an ap-" A knocking sound disrupted their little fight. Agatha managed to reach the door, all to Adeline's disapproval.

    "Come in," a voice appeared from within the office room.

    With a pleased smile, Agatha shot Adeline a look and opened the door in an overly enthusiastic way.

    The secretary immediately held onto the wooden frame and fixed her eyes on Rio.

    "Sorry, Ms. Vidal, I tried to keep her out but she wouldn't-" Of course this bitch would snitch on her the second it was possible. Rio shook her head weakly. "It's alright, I told her earlier that it's fine on my end. I still got some time to spare."

    Agatha threw another triumphant smile at the blonde, who's face was turning red with anger. She backed up and closed the door behind the brunette, leaving the two women to themselves.

    "Your secretary really doesn't budge," Agatha rolled her eyes. Rio sighed. "She values my time more than I do." She leaned back in her chair. "How can I help you?"

    Agatha made her way towards the desk, not even bothering to sit down. She offered the infamous papers to her boss.

    "This document is a mess."

    The CEO took the papers and looked over them. "Mh, Madelaine's report of last month's revenues."

    "No wonder you fired her after commiting such an atrocity." Rio chuckled. "She left because she was finally going into retirement." Agatha cleared her throat and clapped her hands together. "Well, there are quite a few mistakes in this report and I feel like it might've impacted the overall status of the company's current finances within the affected department. You may or may not be missing out on some numbers," she explained, hoping that Rio would simply get the notion that this had to be fixed as soon as possible, no matter how.

    "Have you consulted Mr. Proctor about this issue yet?"

    "I did, but he wasn't exactly helpful."

    "Well, you'd have to restructure the document, I suppose. You know how to do that."

    Agatha closed her eyes for a second. She really had to ask for permission, didn't she?

    "Yes, but no. I don't think it would be very sufficient to do it in your usual ways."

    She tried to put it as simple as possible, giving her attempt at sneaking her way around another chance.

    Rio nodded and Agatha was sure she definitely had her where she wanted her.

    "I don't think I am getting your point just yet."

    The papers left her hands to meet the cool material of the desk. "How exactly do you plan on approaching this, then? Corporate says there's only one way to do it."

    Agatha stood still for a moment.

    "I could give this to someone else, if you want. Maybe they can-" "God, no. Anything but that."

    If there is one thing Agatha absolutely despised, it's seeing parts of work getting absolutely massacred by someone else.

    "How do you plan on solving this, then?"

    Agatha began fidgeting with her hands. Before she could say anything, Rio pointed at the chair opposite of her.

    "Please, have a seat."

    The older woman did as ordered. She tried to construct multiple sentences in her head, none of them sounding adequate enough to Agatha's liking. "Well, given that, in this case, your proposed structure doesn't really work – no offense."

    Rio flung her hands up in a way to show that none was taken. "I wanted to ..."

    Agatha avoided Rio's eye contact.

    "I wanted to ..."

    She started rubbing her fingers along the edge of the desk, suddenly incredibly interested in the way the wooden material was processed. She looked back up at Rio, who was still waiting for an answer. Her face remained neutral. In an instant, Agatha realised that this woman wasn't going to give into her.

    Agatha sighed audibly.

    "I wanted to ask you for permission to work this out in my own way," she blurted out.

    The CEO raised an eyebrow and shifted her face a little to the side. "Sorry, what was that?"

    Oh, she was most definitely toying with her by now. Agatha did not know whether to hate it or find it incredibly alluring. She used to follow these methods as well, back in the day.

    "I wanted to ask you for permission to work this out in my own way," Agatha repeated, this time in a much slower and louder manner. Rio's smile returned to her lips. She was pleased with that answer. "Good. Wasn't so hard, was it?" she cooed.

    Normally, Agatha would've lashed out if anyone had pulled a move like that on her, but she had to give it to Rio. She knew what button to press to get what she wanted, and Agatha decided to like it.

    "You do get my permission."

    Agatha felt a giant weight fall from her chest. The bandaid was off. "However," Rio leaned further towards Agatha. "This is the only report you'll work out with your methods. Your daily tasks will be worked out according to corporate standards."

    Agatha nodded. She opened her mouth to audibly accept the offer, but Rio cut her off beforehand.

    "And, I want you to thoroughly explain your report to me before you turn it in. I want to understand what makes your method so special." "Fine. It's a deal."

    "Eso es lo que me gusta escuchar."

    Rio slid the documents back to her. Her gaze never left the woman in front of her.

    "Is there anything else I can do for you?"

    Agatha looked back and forth between the papers and her boss. "No, that's about it."

    She pushed her chair back a little in order to get ready to leave the office. A moment passed and she suspected that Rio could see the gears in Agatha's brain turning. There still was one thing lingering on her mind, something she had thought about asking some time ago but didn't dare say just yet. Might as well put it all on the table now.

    "Actually, I do have one more question."

    "Yes?"

    Agatha opened her mouth to let her thought be known, when a sudden peeping sound occupied Rio's attention. She glanced at her wristwatch and proceeded to slide her finger over the screen.

    "Sorry, I gotta head to a meeting now. But I can still answer your question, if you like."

    Agatha contemplated for a moment.

    "No, nevermind. It's not that important." She shrugged her shoulders slightly, brushing the thought off.

    The office door opened slightly and Adeline's head popped up from behind. "Ms. Vidal, your meeting with Mr. Stark starts in five minutes", she said. The CEO offered her a nod.

    "I'll be there in a minute."

    Adeline did not leave her position and started staring at Agatha. She could feel the way her eyes bore into her back, urging her to finally stop wasting her precious boss' valuable time.

    Rio's brown eyes met Agatha's blue ones again.

    "You sure?", she whispered, asking for reassurance that she did in fact not want to ask her question right now.

    "Yes, it's fine," she declared and got back on her heels. Agatha made her way towards the door.

    "I'll have the document restructured by Friday. Are you free by 5pm?" she asked, earning an accepting nod from Rio as an answer.

    Agatha continued to walk past Adeline.

    "Make sure to mark it in her appointment calendar," she uttered to the secretary. She gave the blonde one last condescending look before heading back to her cubicle.

    After an exhausting day of trying to fix her predecessor's document, Agatha finally arrived back in her apartment. She immediately jumped in the shower, feeling a great wave of refreshment hit her all at once. When she got out, she made her way to the fridge to devour her leftover food from yesterday.

    Her phone started buzzing again. The screen lit up, revealing her mothers number yet again. The messages started to stack up and occupy the lockscreen. Eventually, curiosity got the best of her and Agatha decided to open Evanora's messages.

    birth giver: You ungrateful child Here we go again, Agatha thought.

    birth giver: Rumours have it that you started working for Vidal Corp., is that correct?!

    birth giver: After everything I have done for you, this is the thanks I get?? After 45 entire years of psychological terror and harassment? Yes, definitely a great way to thank her. Agatha's lips curved up into a small smile. She was pissing her mother off, one goal she strived to achieve when she started her new job. It happened way quicker than anticipated, plastering her with great contentment.

    birth giver: Do you honestly believe you could regain your position somewhere else? Not even Vidal would step so low She hovered her fingers above the keyboard of her phone, ready to type an answer when another message appeared on the screen.

    birth giver: You already messed up, Agatha. You do not deserve another chance. Not even the likes of them would want a failure like you The brunette instinctively turned off the screen. She stared at the black square for a few minutes, before slowly returning to her bowl of food. An entire year of silence, just to hear the same words all over again. After she had finished eating, Agatha got ready for bed and buried herself beneath a thick blanket. Her thoughts wandered for a moment. She was used to her mother's harsh tone by now, given that she never really heard anything else come out of her mouth but deep resentment for Agatha. No matter what she did, no matter how much effort she put in, it would never be enough for her mother. Agatha grew accustomed to it over time, almost growing numb to that feeling of being unwanted by the one who was supposed to love her the most.

    She thought back to the last sent message. Yes, she had indeed messed up back then when everything had become too much. The day she saw through her mother's games and realized she was merely using her own daugther for profit, Agatha finally snapped. Her regular life ended that fateful moment, leaving her with nothing but an empty shell of what once was. Wanda tried to console her after, telling her that life goes on regardless. Throughout the entire course of history, humans have received chances of restarting and finally settling into the life they were always meant to lead. She buried her face in the pillow and closed her eyes.

    Agatha once thought herself worthy of a second chance at life, as well. With every passing day, she felt like she was merely lying to herself at this point.

    Chapter End Notes Translations: Eso es lo que me gusta escuchar = That's what I like to hear Thanks again for reading!

    Chapter 3: Chapter Three Chapter Summary A (temporary) truce is made and an opportunity arises.

    Chapter Notes Hello there, sorry for the delay!

    See the end of the chapter for more notes The days passed by and the reports started piling up. Agatha was working on her daily tasks all the while trying to ignore her mother's last lousy attempt to make her feel bad about herself yet again. Work gave her some distraction and she was more than eager to delve into it, willing to spend even more hours in the office than usual.

    Friday approached in no time. The clock rang 12pm, calling most office workers from Agatha's floor to the cafeteria. She waited for some time in order to complete a few of her tasks in the now almost entirely empty office room. After about fifteen minutes, she decided to leave her cubicle and head down to the cafeteria as well.

    Agatha could still see her Excel table in front of her on her way to the dining area. She used to avoid this place the first few days, but the hunger and curiosity ultimately ended up being stronger today. When she stood in line to grab her lunch, her thoughts reverted back to the reports she would be working on next.

    "What do you want, sweetheart?"

    She suddenly jerked back from whatever plane her soul was floating on. "Come again?"

    An older woman with shoulder-length blonde hair shot Agatha a smile across the kitchen area. She was gesturing towards the food options in front of her, ready to fill a plate to her liking.

    "What do you want to eat?" the lady repeated.

    "Uh ..."

    Agatha waved her hand above the options.

    "Give me ... whatever this is."

    She pointed at the tray with what she could only muster be some form of potato salad.

    The old lady was looking at another tray.

    "This one?", she asked, pointing at the tray to the right of it. "No, this-" Agatha pointed again. "This one."

    The blonde nodded frantically.

    "Ahh, the tomato soup!"

    "No, not the-" Agatha was about to loose her last nerve, to a cafeteria lady of all people. "You know what, just give me whatever."

    "Would you like the vegetarian option? Or vegan one? There's some egg in the potato salad, I'll tell you that-"

    "Just put some food on the plate!"

    The sudden shift in tone visibly startled the older woman a bit. She immediately followed order.

    "Yes, but of course."

    Now, instead of the potato salad, the tomato soup occupied the space on the round porcellain.

    "There you go, darling."

    Agatha threw her a fake smile and accepted her plate.

    "Thank you, Mrs.-"

    She squinted her eyes. The name plate of the cafeteria lady was almost unreadable. Agatha could only recognize the letters H, A and R. "Mrs. Hart."

    It was the next best name she could come up with.

    The woman shook her head and chuckled.

    "No, honey. It's Sharon Davis. Formally, you'd call me Mrs. Davis but you can call me Sharon. That is what most people here tend to go for, anyway. S h a r o n."

    Before Mrs. Davis could recite her entire life story to the brunette, Agatha simply nodded and started heading towards the various tables in the area. There were too many people sitting literally everywhere, so Agatha decided to head straight towards her workplace.

    That was until she saw Billy waving his hand to gain her attention. She tried to play it off and pretend she didn't see him, but when she turned to actually leave this place he started to shout her name.

    "Agatha, over here!"

    The cold shudder of embarassement worked its way up Agatha's skin. A few people started to avert their eyes from their plate, mustering the woman up and down. There was not a single time where a Maximoff wouldn't at least partially ruin her day.

    She made her way towards the desk and sat down in front of him. He offered her a gentle smile.

    "This is the first time we're having a meal together."

    "We've already had plenty of meals together, Billy. I used to feed you, you know."

    Billy laughed. "No, I meant as official co-workers."

    "What a delight. I'll mark it in my calendar so we can celebrate it next year, no?"

    She started fidgeting with her spoon, not really knowing how to eat the tomato soup right now. Why would they ever serve something like this, considering every single person in this company was required to wear a white shirt at work every single day? They are basically asking for ruined clothes.

    She glanced over to Billy, who was stirring his potato salad. Lucky bastard. "You ... don't happen to be interested in a trade, do you?" Billy scrunched his eyebrows. He looked at his chest and then back to Agatha.

    "And risk ruining my white shirt? Nah, I pass."

    A small puff of air left Agatha's mouth.

    "I hope you choke on your potatoes."

    She started digging into her soup with the utmost caution. "I hope that soup burns your tongue off," he shot right back. Another figure slowly made its way toward their shared table. Billy's face started to light up, waving his hand in an inviting gesture yet again. "Jen! Over here!"

    The tall woman walked right past Agatha and placed her plate besides the young man. She sat down and avoided looking at the brunette. "Ow, Kale. There's a free seat besides me, too, you see." Agatha put on her best pout.

    "Can't risk infecting my food with your toxicity, Harkness," Jennifer snarled.

    "Oh my god, guys." Billy was getting tired of it already. "Please, can't you two just make up or something? At least during work hours." "Technically, this is not considered a working hour," Agatha declared, earning an intense stare from both of them.

    Jennifer started picking up potatoes with her fork because of course she would also be eating the salad instead of that stupid fucking tomato soup. "I might consider it when she apologizes to me."

    Agatha started laughing.

    "An apology? For what? You were doing a shitty job and faced the consequences, there's no need for an apology."

    "I made one simple mistake and then even proceeded to fix it. It was not much of a big deal," Jen explained.

    The two of them started to discuss and talk over one another like little kids fighting over who gets to play with a toy. Their bantering already started to attract the attention of some people sitting close to their table. "Okay, okay – guys!"

    Billy waved his hands in front of the women like a referee. "Let's just find some kind of compromise. Jen, what do you want?" "An apology."

    "Agatha?"

    "For her to stop being so upset over a completely logical decision." Billy blinked a few times. This was not going to work.

    "Alright, well, it's clear that you are the root of the problem, Agatha." She threw her hands in the air.

    "When am I not," she sighed.

    "I think you should just apologize to her. It's not that hard," the boy insisted, receiving an affirmative nod from Jen.

    Agatha moved her eyes from one person to another.

    "I'd rather die than-"

    "See, I told you. She's nothing but a coward."

    Jen muttered her words loud enough for the other woman to hear. The word coward cut deep.

    "I'm anything but a coward."

    Jen put her fork down, crossing her arms in front of her chest. "Then prove it."

    "Okay!"

    She arched her back, preparing her body for what she's about to say. "I'm sorry," she mumbled unintelligibly.

    Jennifer and Billy looked at each other and then returned their gaze back to Agatha. She was fiddling the edges of the table, waiting for a response. She said sorry. What more did they want to hear?

    "I don't believe you," is all Jen delivered as a reaction. Billy's followed as well.

    "That wasn't genuine, Agatha."

    "You're never happy with anything, are you?"

    A moment passes. They were staring holes into her by now. "Fine, have it your way. Jennifer Kale, I am sorry for getting you fired all those years ago. I deeply regret my decision and can only hope you'll be able to forgive me one day."

    Agatha tried her best to make it sound as believable as possible; luckily for her, theatrics were part of her strongest suits.

    The woman with the buzzcut narrowed her eyes. It was not exactly what she wanted to hear and she still didn't believe it to be genuine, but she could work with it. At least for the meantime.

    "I'll think about accepting your apology. Give me a few days. Or weeks." "Send me an email to let me know;" Agatha exhaled deeply and rolled her eyes.

    Billy was content with the peace that fell upon them (at least, for the moment, which was infinitely better than nothing).

    "See? Wasn't so hard after all."

    The three of them sat in silence for a few minutes until Agatha caught a glimpse of Adeline standing at the buffet of the cafeteria. She was having an overly enthusiastic conversation with Mrs. Hart – sorry, Sharon. Billy followed the brunette's gaze. Jennifer was still occupied with her food. "Do you know what's up with her?" Agatha asked, hoping to get some answers out of him.

    "With whom? Ms. Reed?"

    Agatha bobbed her head.

    The boy furrowed his brows in confusion.

    "She's quite alright. A bit loud sometimes but nothing I'd consider off." He put a potato in his mouth. "She's definitely nicer than you are." "Oh come on, that's not hard. No need to give her so much credit for that." Billy looked back at Agatha, then back to Adeline. Eventually, his gaze settled back on the brunette.

    "Why are you asking? You like her?" he smirked. The comment made Jen look up from her plate as well.

    Agatha's face was painted with horror.

    "Fuck no, I need her to stay away from me as far as possible. Might as well start carrying a spray bottle with me to keep her at distance." Sharon handed Adeline exactly two cups of coffee. They continued to talk for a little longer until the young woman started to retreat from the counter. Agatha figured exactly who the other cup was for.

    She turned her body back to the boy in front of her. "Are she and Ms. Vidal ... you know."

    Billy almost actually choked on his potato. The next thing he did was try to muster up a sentence in between caughs.

    "What ... like, dating?"

    Agatha widened her eyes, waiting for an actual confirmation. She waved her hands around in order to urge Billy to simply spit out a yes or no. The poor boy was still focused on not losing his life to a vegetable. "What is it, are they dating or not?" she asked again.

    "No," Jen interjected. "No, they're not."

    Agatha felt a sudden wave of emotion surge over her. It felt close to relief, but instead of investigating the feeling any further, she decided to ignore it completely.

    Right after regaining his composure, Billy leaned forwards a bit, inviting Agatha to do the same.

    "But rumours say that Ms. Reed has a crush on her."

    That would explain her overly obsessive behaviour. Agatha knew that personal assistants could be all up in your personal space, but Adeline Reed? She was basically stuck to Rio's feet at all times. Jen was clearly fighting back a smirk. "Well, if you're not after Reed, then

    Billy's eyes suddenly lit up with realisation. "Oh my god, you're into Ms. Vidal?"

    Agatha frantically shook her head. "No. I am not interested in her like that." The young man slowly bobbed his head in aknowledgement, although he didn't quite believe her statement. Jennifer took part in the exact opposite motion, swaying hers from side to side.

    "You know, I can't blame you. She is a good looking woman," Kale said. Agatha smirked.

    "Alright then. Go ahead and shoot your shot, Jen."

    "Nope, I already have a girlfriend." Jennifer's face lit up at the mention of her partner. "The shot's all yours. Not that I'd wish her the misfortune of having to keep up with you."

    "Yeah, I mean, she's your type, is she not?" Billy shot in. He really was Wanda's son.

    "She's – she's not my-"

    Agatha got tired of their constant blabber regarding her preferences. She continued eating her soup without resuming what she initially wanted to say. It had no use whatsoever.

    Rio was indeed very much her type, but Agatha hated to admit that both Wanda and Billy were right about it all along.

    Clicking sounds of high heels crept their way up to the group. Before they knew it, Adeline was standing right in front of their table. A smile was plastered on her lips, making her dips even more visible. The group collectively looked up at her.

    "Greetings everyone, hope you enjoy your lunch."

    She placed the two coffee cups on the table and proceeded to take out a little notebook alongside a shiny black pen.

    "Ms. Harkness, regarding your appointment with Ms. Vidal," she started out. "5pm today is indeed managable. Would you mind signing this as a confirmation that you'll be there on time?"

    Agatha's eyebrows furrowed. That woman took her job way too seriously. She reached out for the notebook and pen the blonde was offering her. Before it could enter her hands, a sudden 'plop' sound filled her ears, followed by a series of gasps from the others at the table. She looked down to her plate, finding the notebook drenched in the soup. The impact caused it to splatter all over Agatha's shirt, staining the white cloth with multiple red spots. This fucking-

    "Oh my god, I am so sorry!!" Adeline exclaimed, trying to scramble her notebook out of the red mass. Jennifer and Billy watched as the situation unfolded, quick in realising Agatha's furiosity. The brunette further inspected her shirt and started to back up from the table. "Sorry, let me just-" Adeline cleaned up the notebook first and then offered the red napkin to Agatha.

    The older woman shot her a glance. If looks could kill, the secretary would've dropped dead in an instant, right in the middle of the cafeteria. She wanted to shout at her, put her in her place in front of all the people sitting around them, but she knew exactly that this is what Adeline also expected. This girl clearly seemed to hate her, and Agatha would only fall right into her trap if she were to overreact right now. It would only result in getting sent back to her boss, getting scolded and possibly be put at risk of losing her job. She could not afford that, not again.

Both Jennifer and Billy started helping her out, before the latter decided to run after Agatha. She had already disappeared into one of the closest bathrooms, trying her best to get the soup off her shirt.

All of her efforts evaporated into nothingness when lunch break was over and Agatha had no other choice but to return to the office. She was sure to bring a back up shirt the next time. The hours passed and the moment the last Excel table was written, everything finally fell into place. The last piece of the puzzle made way to show off that there was indeed a miscalculation regarding last month's revenues. Agatha plopped her head onto the table with a loud thud. Her body slumped in relief.

    Agatha let out a quiet laugh as she made her way to claim the unoccupied chair.

In the end, she decided not to. Her boss' secretary was messing with her, that was no secret to Agatha anymore. But if she were to give into her schemes, things might turn out for the blondes favor and Agatha could afford anything but that outcome. The older woman was hot headed at times, but even after all those years, she did not lose the ability to make calculated moves.

    Rio nodded in aknowledgement.

    "Well, no need to worry about it", she said, shifting her position in the chair. Agatha watched her every move. Once Rio had settled into a comfortable position, she nudged her head slightly.

    "Red looks good on you."

    Blue orbs met hazel ones. Agatha was a bit taken aback by what Rio said, expecting anything but that. Heat began wandering up her cheeks; her skin suddenly painted with a soft shade of pink.

    "You flatter me"," she said playfully, earning a small chuckle from the other woman.

    Agatha turned her focus back on her document, the comment still lingering in the back of her mind.

    "Alright, Ms. Vidal-"

    "Rio."

    "Pardon?"

    "Please, call me Rio."

    "Alright" Agatha smiled. "Rio."

    Hearing her name fall from Agatha's lips with such ease sent a pleasant shiver down the CEO's spine. She grazed her tongue slightly against the inside of her cheek and followed Agatha's gaze towards the report. "I switched around a few dates to follow another structure. It stacks the investments in a more logical order, giving better insight where they ultimately ended up at the end of the month."

    She turned the papers around so Rio could read through them thoroughly. Agatha continued to explain her methods to her, shifting her finger over the papers to show what she was talking about. Rio nodded along in order to let the woman know she was catching on.

    Agatha continued to ramble on, thoroughly explaining her methods to the woman in front of her. She has missed this. Despite being in a lower position, she finally got to revel in her element and apply some of the knowledge she has gathered all throughout her life. Accounting wasn't her most favourite element by far, but it did her justice in this moment. An entire year of agonizingly crushing jobs have led her to at least a tiny bit of satisfaction in this newfound atmosphere.

    They ended up discussing her methods and the benefits they could potentially bring for the daily tasks the accounting departmant was performing. More time passed and the brunette felt her mouth become dry little by little.

    "Coffee?" Rio asked after hearing her counterpart clear her throat multiple times, handing Agatha one of the coffee cups.

    She eyed the drink with concern.

    "I'm afraid your secretary would try to poison me."

    Rio's mouth curved upwards slightly.

    "Adeline has already left, I got these myself. Definitely not poisoned." The woman nodded in return, taking the coffee cup and taking a sip. Rio followed after her with her own.

    "Did anything occur between the two of you?" she asked.

    Agatha took another sip, bigger this time. She sat the coffee back on the table.

    "We just had a little misunderstanding. That's all."

    "I see. If there ever is a problem though, don't hesitate to let me know." The older woman bobbed her head in order to to let Rio know that she understood.

    "I can handle myself."

    She suddenly felt the soft touch of a hand on her arm. Agatha's gaze met Rio's in an instant, not quite knowing how to react to this motion. A stern expression met the fazed one plastered on Agatha's face.

    "I know you're capable of that, but I do mean it. You don't have to handle everything yourself, Ms. Harkness."

    She gave her arm a soft squeeze.

    It took Agatha some time to adjust to this sudden proximity. The corner of her mouth moved upwards.

    "Agatha," she said. "Please, call me Agatha."

    Rio grinned at the way the other woman mimicked her tone. She gave her an understanding nod.

    "Gladly."

    Rio added more pressure to the fingers which were currently occupying part of Agatha's arm.

    "Agatha."

    The name was barely above a whisper, adding another layer to the sensation of the prolonged physical touch.

    "Formidable," Agatha whispered.

    Rio got up from her chair, grabbing some papers from a cupboard not too far away from the two of them. She returned to Agatha's side and handed her the documents. A triumphant smile was plastered on the accountant's lips.

    "I'll make sure they're already set for you."

    They stood a little closer together than before. Agatha made notice of Rio's fragrance that still managed to hold up even after a long, tedious work day. It had a rather floral scent to it; not too sweet. It reminded her of colourful flower fields, left in the wake of a sudden surge of summer rain. "Thanks for the coffee," Agatha remarked before making her way towards the office door.

    She felt Rio's gaze linger on her and turned to meet it before stepping out of the room. The younger woman opened her mouth to say something, just to be cut off by her accountant.

    "Te veo," Agatha uttered, before stepping into the hallway. Rio was now left stranded in her office, accompanied by nothing but blooming thoughts and empty coffee cups.
