    Rio's expression grew more stern.

    "I need you to leave some work for the others as well. There are enough people in the accounting compartment that can finish those reports aside from you."

    A puff of air left Agatha's mouth. It all sounded so pathetic to her. All of a sudden, she was the one doing the dirty work, completing tasks that were very much beneath her. Her annoyance grew with every single thought she spent on looking at her wasted potential, rotting away in a much lower paid position. And now, her boss, something she barely even had back then, was scolding her for it.

    "Please, that's no feat for me. I can complete them in half the time." "What I said was not a question, Agatha."

    The accountant cut off their eye contact. As much as she wished it to, she had no power over this whatsoever. She watched as the light turned green again, the car moving in its tracks.

    "As your boss, I need you to understand that you can not simply do everything on your own accord. I have spoken to Mr. Proctor about this as well and he won't give you any more additional reports for the time being, at least not without my approval."

    Rio's eyes followed the direction of the street. She saw Agatha nodding in the corner of her eyes, clearly unhappy with her decision. "What's going on, Agatha?" she asked, her voice much more gentle than before. The sudden switch made the older woman quickly glance back at her.

    "What do you mean?"

    "You seem to be a little absentminded as of lately."

    "I got to focus a lot on work, that's all."

    "A lot of work, that you burdened upon yourself, that is. People usually dive into more tasks in order to avoid something."

    Agatha looked back out the window. She let the silence dwell for a moment before speaking up again.

    "I don't really want to talk about that right now."

    Rio nodded and shifted the gear. They were now seated at the next red light, patiently awaiting the colour to turn back to green.

    "I understand," she said. "But if you ever feel like it, just know that you can talk to me anytime."

    Agatha moved her head in the CEO's direction. The tone of her voice and the soft glaze of her hazel eyes made Agatha believe that Rio really meant it. It felt strangely personal; this wasn't just a boss talking to their employee anymore.

    They lock eyes for a moment. Rio's fingers started grazing the shifting gear again, accidentally brushing Agatha's thigh. The other didn't flinch away, but instead intensified their shared gaze further.

    A sudden growl erupting from Agatha's stomach interrupted the moment, sending Rio into a tiny fit of laughter.

    "My, someone's hungry."

    Agatha couldn't stifle her laugh either. "I might've skipped lunch today," she admitted.

    The light turned green, causing the car to carry on again. Rio made a turn not too long after.

    "Wanna grab a bite?" she asked Agatha.

    "I don't want to hold you up, you're already doing a lot for me right now." "Oh no, I mean it. Besides, I don't really feel like cooking, either." Another turn. This was certainly not the direction Agatha had given her some time prior.

    "I know a place," Rio continued.

    "Looks like I don't really have a choice, then," Agatha grinned, accepting her fate. She heard Rio chuckle in return as said woman grabbed the steering wheel tighter.

    Shortly after, they found themselves parked close to a tiny asian restaurant. If Agatha had ever driven down this street before, she would have surely never recognized this place herself.

    Rio held the door for her as she entered the building. She was suddenly hit with various delicious smells, feeling her stomach react in return. "That's not what I expected," Agatha said as she mustered the place further. A few people were occupying the seats sprawled across the tight space. "It may not look like a five star restaurant, but I swear, the food here is good. I come here pretty often," Rio said, fixing her blazer in the process. She looked oddly out of place in her attire, but so did Agatha. "Well, got any suggestions then?"

    "Sure. You like it tame or spicy?"

    After ordering their meals, Rio having the usual and Agatha revealing that she indeed hated spicy food and therefore going with Rio's safest option, they both sat down and waited for their order to be ready. There was a booth close to the window left unoccupied, giving the both of them a cozy space to relapse after work. They sat across each other, Agatha impatiently tapping her nails on the desk and Rio leaning back on the rest of the seat, laying her arm atop of it.

    "About your reports ..." Agatha blurted out, being faced with an immediate shake of Rio's head.

    "Let's not talk about work anymore, please. It's been a long day." Agatha nodded. The tapping of her fingers increased as she felt Rio's stare drill itself into her. What was she supposed to talk about aside from work? Smalltalk wasn't Agatha's best suit. She'd usually avoid it if not absolutely necessary.

    "I'm curious," she started out, unsure where this conversation would even allow itself to go. "Do you take every employee of yours to your favourite restaurant?"

    "Only the special ones," Rio said with a wink.

    The action sent a tiny, but pleasant shock through Agatha's veins. "You've really never been here before?" Rio asked.

    Agatha shook her head. "No, I didn't even know they had a restaurant in this quarter of the city."

    "This spot is pretty famous for those who want to grab a bite after a party. There's a lot of clubs and bars around here."

    "Is that the reason why you eat here so often?"

    Rio smiled faintly. "It used to be one, but I haven't been to a party in ages." Agatha was met with several imaginary scenarios, most of them showing her boss dance her heart out in the middle of a crowded club. A dress adorning her figure, her hair flowing in the rhythm of carefully placed steps. Oddly enough, the image fit the woman. Despite her being the CEO of one of the biggest companies in the city, Rio seemed like the person to go out often and meet people on her own accord.

    "Why did you stop going?" Agatha asked curiously.

    "Work got in the way, mostly. I may have also gotten a bit too old for parties, anyway."

    Agatha scoffed loudly.

    "Too old? You can't be serious."

    "I'm 34."

    "Okay, now I'm offended," Agatha huffed all the while laying a hand on her chest. "You're practically fresh out the womb. Go ahead and have some fun, you still have your whole life ahead of you."

    Rio laughed, showing her white canines in the process. Her gaze moved to the ordering booth of the restaurant for a short moment before turning back to Agatha.

    "I'll think about it."

    The owner of the restaurant shouted two numbers through the building, indicating that their food was now ready. Rio shifted in her seat, ready to get the order when Agatha stood up and urged the CEO to stay in place. "You're driving me. Let me at least cover the food," she said before grabbing her purse from her bag and strutting off to the booth. Once the meals were paid for and brought to the table, the two of them dived right in. Agatha's stomach stopped complaining, the comfort of its silence engulfing the woman instantly.

    "How long have you been living outside the city, if I may ask?" Rio asked after picking up some of the food with her fork.

    "Just about four months. I used to live with a friend of mine for some time until I found my current apartment."

    "Do you like it there?"

    "I'm still getting used to it, to be honest. I've been surrounded by skyscrapers my whole life, it certainly feels different. It's a little too quiet to my liking."

    Rio nodded.

    "I've always liked the thought of getting out of the city," she said. "Is the long driving distance bugging you?"

    "That's not the problem," Rio stopped eating for a moment. "I'm bound to the company. Always on call, always close to the source. You know what I am talking about, don't you?"

    Agatha nodded, thinking back to the days at Harkness Corp.. The hours she spent there piled up to be more than she could take a hold of, bothering her not in the slightest. Her workplace was her home, the one thing she could always come back to and that would always come back to her. Agatha's past apartment used to reside pretty close to the complex, giving her the possibility to shift her worklife to her liking.

    "I do. But given that you're the CEO of your company, you could handle those things to your preference."

    "That's not really the case here."

    "Why not?"

    "Although I am officially the CEO of Vidal Corp, part of the company still belongs to my parents. I am not only bound to certain corporate rules, when it comes to your request about the accounting guidelines for example, but I am also partially bound to them."

    Agatha nodded and slurped down a few more of her own noodles. "And you'd like to change that?"

    "One day, eventually."

    "Let me give you one advice, try not to stretch it out too long and start working on it as soon as possible. I am the living evidence for why it's a bad decision to stay contracted to your own parents."

    "You were supposed to be CEO, were you not?" Rio asked curiously, hoping that it wouldn't evoke any additional negative feelings in Agatha. "Yes, I was," Agatha poked around in her meal, gathering a few more noodles on it and putting them in her mouth straight away. "Make sure you work it out, or you'll end up just like me."

    "You've achieved a lot in your life. I'm sure most people envy that." "Please, all I am now is a simple accountant, living in an flat that is short from falling apart at any given minute."

    "Where you are right now does not dismiss all that you have managed to achieve until this point. You shouldn't discredit yourself like that, Agatha," Rio said.

    Agatha's eating motions stopped for a mere moment. She didn't know how to respond to Rio's words, so she decided to stay silent and let her mind soak them up like a sponge.

    "Can I tell you a secret?" the CEO continued after a few seconds of silence. The corners of her mouth moved up slightly, anticipating Agatha's answer giddily.

    "Mh, go ahead. I love secrets," Agatha answered with a smirk. She leaned closer to Rio, who nudged her head to the side.

    "Do you remember the 2011 Finance Summit Conference in Toronto?" The accountant's eyes lit up instantly. Of course she remembered. It was one of the biggest conferences she has had the honour to present at, showing off a new financial model to one of the largest crowds she was met with in her entire life.

    "I could never forget that day. It's one of the most memorable days of my life, considering only the best had the chance to present at this conference." Rio leaned back a bit, waiting for Agatha's further reaction. She was met with wider eyes than before, and a finger pointing at her accusingly. "You were there, too," Agatha mustered.

    "I was," the woman admitted, smiling at the thought of her former self sitting in the middle of the large crowd. "Back then, I was still in the middle of my studies. A friend of mine and I had to attend the conference and I thought it to be pretty boring until it was your turn to present. You had me hooked."

    Agatha slumped back in her seat. She couldn't believe what she was hearing right now.

    "I don't think I saw you, back then."

    "I'm sure you didn't. And even if you did, that was thirteen years ago, I looked a lot different," Rio collected the last bit of her meal and put it in her mouth. "I was blonde at that time, too."

    "Well, that is a surprise."

    Rio's head bobbed.

    "Ever since then, I have followed your work for years. You were a huge inspiration throughout my studies, and the years to come." "My, who would've thought that my own boss was such a huge fan of my work?" Agatha smirked, cocking her eyebrow simultaneously. A thought shot through Agatha's head all of a sudden. The question that had lingered on her mind for a very long time now, ever since the first interview to be exact, popped up without hesitation. It seemed like the dots were finally connecting, but she needed to hear the answer fall from Rio's own lips.

    She squinted her eyebrows and fixated her gaze on the younger woman. "Is that the reason why you hired me?" she asked.

    Rio stayed silent for a few seconds before she decided to give an answer. "Partially."

    She leaned back in the seat, her meal finished now as well. Her chest heaved heavily, her hazel orbs meeting blue ones more intensely than before.

    "I mainly hired you because you asked for a job, and I need someone highly competent to fulfill it."

    "Although I got fired from my own company, you thought it would be a good idea to give me a chance at yours?"

    The two of them didn't break their gaze. Rio felt that Agatha was searching for something, intentionally or not.

    "I did. I felt like you deserved this chance," Rio smiled gently. "And I was right. You do."

    Agatha felt her heart clench. This was the exact opposite of what she had heard her mother say previously. Of all the people she had ever thought of, Agatha would've never expected her own economic rival to be the one to push her work forwards. It was an unusual thought, but she welcomed it with a warm feeling brooding inside of her.

    They sat in silence for a bit before Rio decided to break it. "Wanna go home?" she asked.

    Agatha answered without words, making her head move up and down instead. She swung her bag over her shoulder and met Rio's height as they got up from their seats. The both of them grabbed the leftover dishes and brought them back to where they got them from.

    This time, it was Agatha who opened the door for Rio.

    "You were right, the food was pretty good," she said while following her in her tracks.

    "I'm glad you liked it."

    They entered the car again, Agatha shifting in her seat to find a comfortable position.

    "Alright then, no more kidnapping. Where do you live again, exactly?" Rio asked.

    After giving her the directions, Rio made her way towards the destination. Some time after, they arrived at an apartment complex outside of the city, only a few of the windows still lit. The neighborhood was quiet, indicating that most people had gone to sleep by now.

    Once the car came to a stop, Agatha turned to Rio.

    "Thank you for driving me," she said.

    Rio gave her one of her signature soft smiles.

    "Thank you for paying for the food."

    Agatha opened the door and got out of the car. Before she closed it, she bent lower to look back at Rio.

    "Get home safe, Rio," she said before closing the door.

    She saw her boss wave at her from within the car, Agatha returning the motion without thinking too much about it. She walked towards the building, reaching her apartment in no time.

    Once she was inside, she took off her shoes and blazer, moving her body towards the living room and putting her bag on the desk. She rummaged around in it, taking out some of her work utensils and sorting them for the next shift. When her hands grabbed one of her notebooks, she realized that there was something else stuck to it.

    She took a hold of what looked like a napkin. Agatha thought it must've accidentally fallen into her bag when she was at the restaurant, so she decided to throw it away.

    Before her hand let go of it, she realized that something was written on the material. She folded it in order to read what it said.

    To keep me updated about your work – or to hit me up if you ever feel like partying with a newborn Right beneath the message was a lined up order of numbers. Agatha smirked at the realization that this was the way Rio would be giving her her private number. She must've sneaked the napking into her bag when Agatha went to pay for the food.

    Agatha got ready for bed before deciding to text her boss. She positioned herself under the sheets and grabbed her phone.

    1 new message from birth giver She contemplated for a second, before swiping the notification away. Instead, she dialed the number on the napkin and opened the chat. Agatha: very cheesy way of giving someone your number The status of the message turned to 'read' quickly.

    Rio: but it worked just fine ;)

    Before Agatha could think about it, her finger hovered over the 'add to contacts' button. Once Rio was now officially part of her list, she saw Rio's profile picture appear. She decided to click on it, being met with a picture of Rio wearing something that came close to a jersey, a pair of sunglasses and a cap. She showed a middle finger to whoever was taking said picture. It was certainly a side of the woman Agatha hasn't seen just yet. She couldn't help but feel a little excited about the possibility of getting to know it further.

    Rio: I got home safely, by the way Agatha: I'm glad to hear that Agatha's fingers hovered over the keyboard. After some time of contemplating, she wrote another message.

    Agatha: thank you for today Rio: Anytime She was ready to lay her phone back on her bedside table, when it vibrated again. She was glad to be met with her boss' name again, seeing that she sent another message.

    Rio: sleep well Agatha, I hope your bed is more comfortable than our desks Agatha laughed and typed a message in reply.

    Agatha: good night, Rio It was simple, but felt like something huge to her. She watched as Rio reacted to her message with a sleepy smiley before turning the screen of her smartphone black. It landed on the desk shortly after.

    Agatha grabbed her sheets and buried herself further beneath them, the warmth of them not quite accumulating to what she had felt multiple times throughout this day.

    For the first time in weeks, she easily drifted off into a peaceful sleep. Chapter End Notes (Translations: Despierta, bella durmiente = Wake up, sleeping beauty)

    Rio finally clocked Agatha's private number (or, is it the other way around?).

    Chapter 5: Chapter Five

    Chapter Summary Agatha goes on a quest and meets someone from Rio's past. Chapter Notes Happy New Year everyone!

    I hope you had nice holidays and started off the year well :) See the end of the chapter for more notes Rio's hand shifted over the columns of a document, wandering down to the portion where a signature was required. With a swift motion, her name turned visible on the paper. Not too long after, it joined the pile of documents right beside her.

    The woman's concentration ceased when her phone rang unexpectedly. She looked at it and was met with her mother's name. There it was. Rio decided to curse Hela for speaking this into existence.

    "Hola, mami," Rio said with a rather high pitched voice.

    "¿Te importaría explicar tus acciones?"

    "I'm doing fine, thank you for asking," she remarked in a sarcastic tone. She heard her mother sigh at the other end of the line.

    "What exactly were you thinking about hiring Evanora Harkness' daughter? Are you out of your mind?"

    Rio leaned back in her seat, crossing her legs and fiddling the armrest with her unoccupied hand.

    "She has a name, you know. Agatha was looking for a job, and I decided to give it to her."

    There was no response for a second. Rio could tell that her mother must've been shaking her head in regards to her answer.

    "Have you thought about what that could possibly entail for the company, for our reputation? Where do you think we'd end up if we continued hiring anyone who actively worked against us before?"

    "Well, as far as I can see, she's doing a great job for this company now. I see nothing wrong with that."

    "I highly urge you to think logically about this," her mother started out, "Harkness Corp's revenue has significantly decreased not too long ago. It just so happens to be aligned close with the timeframe of her leaving the company behind."

    "Are you insinuating that she only applied to manipulate my company? No puedes hablar en serio."

    "Rio, economics are a harsh field of constant pushing and pulling. People would do anything to keep their business going, they'd even dare to go way beyond grey areas if it meant further elevating it. I have told you about this so many times already," her mother explained.

    "I don't think her to be someone like that."

    "Do you know her, personally?"

    Rio looked down on the papers she still had to sign. She in fact did not know Agatha that well when it came to her as a person. Not yet, anyways. "That's not important right now," she said before returning to her trail of thoughts, "She has potential, and I knew it would be beneficial for the revenues of our company. You should see the enthusiasm she's working on her tasks with."

    "There's no doubt in whether she has potential or not. Her name's known for a reason, but so are the methods she used to further push her family business forward. What I am simply asking of you is to listen to my advice, and consider undoing your decision before it ends up badly for the company."

    The brunette scrunched her forehead in anger. She couldn't believe what she just heard.

    "You're urging me to disband her because of a bunch of rumours?" "Rio, debes poner tu empresa por delante de tus propios intereses." Her furiosity only grew stronger by the minute. She got up in a hurried motion, walking to the windows of her office.

    "I don't want you to question my actions, mom. I am the representative CEO of this company and it is also me who gets to decide who gets hired or not. Mere rumours won't change my mind, I know exactly what I am doing" "While rumours may not always turn out to be true, I can't allow you putting the company at such a high risk."

    Her mother's voice scratched Rio's ear in an uneasy way. Everytime she thought she had gotten used to her tone, her mother proves otherwise. "According to my reports, we are doing exceptionally well. Revenues have only increased the past months, and that hasn't changed ever since I hired her. She could be an actual game changer if I further push her forwards, it will only ever benefit our business."

    "¿Acaso te escuchas a ti mismo hablando? You pushing her forward into a promising position might actually be exactly what she's after, and one day, she'll use that to her advantage. Evanora's methods have always been questionable, I'm convinced her own blood and flesh doesn't come short in her ways."

    "She is her own person. Doesn't surprise me that you of all people still can't manage to differentiate that."

    "Don't start this topic again, Rio. It has nothing to do with the current situation."

    Rio sighed loudly. She closed her eyes for a moment to collect herself before staring out of the window again.

    "I have to attend a conference now. This conversation is far from over"," her mother said.

    "I sure wish it was," the brunette uttered unintelligibly. "I'll call you back as soon as possible. In the meantime, think about what I said. Si no actúas, intervendré yo mismo."

    Once her mother had hung up, Rio lowered the phone. She watched as the cars outside of the company complex were fighting their way through the traffic. It eased her mind for a brief moment, when the vibration of her phone called back for her attention.

    1 new message from Agatha Reading her employees name made her calm down a little more. She clicked on the notification and opened the chat.

    Agatha: I got an update on your reports. Got to spare some time? Rio moved her fingers quickly over the keyboard.

    Rio: sure do Agatha: Good. Meet up at your office?

    Rio: I'd actually prefer if we could go somewhere else. I need to leave this room or else I'll explode Agatha: Cafeteria?

    Rio: I'll be there in five Shortly after, Rio entered the cafeteria. Lunch break had started a few minutes ago, and the room gradually filled with more people. While greeting a few of them, she walked over to a tiny, round desk a little farther away from the crowd, where a certain brunette was sitting at. A document was lying in front of her, accompanied by two coffee cups. Fingers curled themselves around one of them, the cup moving to Agatha's mouth. "Hey," Rio huffed as she sat besides Agatha.

    The accountant gave her a warm smile.

    "Hey there," she said and gestured towards the other cup, "I got you some coffee."

    Rio instantly took a hold of it, taking a big sip.

    "How did you know I liked my coffee black?" Rio inquired. Agatha looked over to the cafeteria booth. She quickly pointed a finger at it before looking at the documents again.

    "I asked Mrs. Hart to get me the one you usually receive." Rio paused for a moment, not sure who Agatha was referring to. "Mrs. Hart?"

    "The cafeteria lady."

    "Oh, Mrs. Davis." Rio followed Agatha's gaze towards the document. "How come you call her Mrs. Hart?"

    Agatha only shook her head. "That's a long story."

    The accountant started updating her boss about the document, explaining how far she is in completing the reports. She has already outlined a few proposals on financial investments, giving Rio an idea of how it could potentially look like once the documents were finished.

    "You're sure you can complete all of that in time?" she asked. Agatha bobbed her head in return.

    "Absolutely. I have to further outline the proposals and make some additional calculations, but they will be done right in time for the meetup." "That's great. I'll inform Mr. Proctor about your participation, you two will work out on how to present your results later on," Rio said. The two of them shared a silent moment, where Agatha took the opportunity to look Rio up and down. It wasn't hard to notice that she appeared a little distraught. Unsure of whether to approach the topic or not, Agatha fiddled around with a corner of the paper.

    "Stressful day?" she asked the younger woman eventually.

    Rio looked down on her lap. Her fingers tightly pressed against the coffee cup, absorbing the warmth of the liquid that resided in it. "Not exactly," she replied. "My mother called me. She's not very content with an action of mine regarding the business."

    Agatha stayed quiet as to let her continue stating the problem, eyeing the younger woman with attention.

    "She insists on me changing my decision, but I can't follow through. It just ... wouldn't be right."

    Agatha thought back to the conversation they had at the restaurant not too long ago. Rio was bound to her parents, just like Agatha herself was bound to her mother. It seemed like they really did share a sentiment. "You're a CEO for a reason. I'm sure you can figure this out, without having to adhere to the needs of your mother. Trust me, I would know," Agatha declared with a faint smile.

    Rio nodded in response, feeling a sense of comfort squirming its way around her heart.

    "After all, success also stems from the people who lead. You're only 34 and your company is one of the highest grossing ones in the country. I'm sure that whatever you decided to do, you put enough thought into it beforehand. If it feels right to you, it is most likely right for the company." Rio absorbed her words silently.

    "Thank you," she whispered.

    Agatha carefully placed a hand on Rio's back, rubbing it gently. She wasn't sure how the other would react to the action, but ultimately felt herself come to ease when she noticed Rio not fighting against it. Quite in the contrary, the woman seemed to lean further into the touch. Deep blue orbs locked with hazel ones.

    The world seemed to stop around her, voices from colleagues fanning out. All she could see were those big, brown eyes looking at her in the most intimate way she has felt them linger on her yet. Agatha didn't know whether she was imagining it or not, given that her mind felt insanely hazy right now, but she could have sworn that those orbs drifted down to her lips for a short sequence of time. The thought of it sent an excited bolt through her entire body, all the way to her fingertips, which were still resting on the back of Rio's upper body.

    "Agatha," a voice called out to her. It wasn't Rio's, rest assured. "Sorry, Agatha?"

    She tapped into reality shortly after. Her hand left the warm body besides her instantly, as her gaze wandered to the other side of the table. Her eyes stranded on Billy, who was standing in front of the table, looking a little chaotic. She moved her head from side to side, letting long brown locks readjust themselves, all the while regaining her former composure. "I'm so sorry for interrupting, but, do you have a moment?" he issued. Rio turned back to drinking her coffee in silence.

    "What is it?" Agatha scoffed.

    "Sorry, Miss Vidal. Is it okay if I sit down for a second?" Billy asked, grabbing a chair nearby before even gaining an answer.

    "I don't mind. We were done here, anyways," she said, giving him a gentle smile. She leaned back in her chair as Billy slightly hunched over the desk, staring at Agatha in the process.

    "I need your help."

    "What's the problem, kid? Don't tell me you put the wrong documents in the shredder again," Agatha sighed.

    "No, it's far worse."

    "Oh, what the hell did you do!?"

    He pulled out a little notebook and a pen.

    "Alice and I are in dire need to find a location for Jen's birthday party. The one we originally booked called me a few minutes ago, saying that they'd have to cancel the reservation because there was a pipe leak and they don't know if the issue will be fixed til next week."

    Agatha held still for a moment before erupting in a loud fit of laughter. "You can't be serious right now. That is your big problem?" "Her birthday is only a week away, Agatha! We don't have any time left and most instances need a reservation much longer beforehand." "And why exactly are you asking me for this? I don't care where Jen celebrates her birthday, let her do it at home or something. Decorate it with some soap and shampoo bottles and voila, you got the perfect location," she said and took a great sip of her coffee.

    "No, no, Agatha. We can't just celebrate at home."

    "Why not?"

    "I promised her to plan the party at a cool location."

    "And now you want me to do, what, conjure one out of thin air? I'm not a witch, Billy, despite what most people say."

    Billy started fiddling with his notebook. He felt Rio's eyes linger on him as well, his nervousness only growing stronger.

    "You surely know about other bars or clubs around here, do you not? You've been living here most of your life, longer than both Jen and Alice do, you got to know something," he asked Agatha desperately.

    "Ugh, Billy. I have barely been at any parties or bars around here." The young man sighed and looked down at his empty note. The pen twirled around between his fingers multiple times.

    "May I throw something in?" Rio's voice appeared.

    Billy looked up again, nodding frantically. Agatha turned her gaze towards her as well, and of course, Rio would be the one to be the saviour of the situation. They've talked about parties before, and Rio has been to a lot of them, at least some time ago. She'd know exactly where to go. "Yes, please"," Billy spoke, leaning forwards to hear Rio's upcoming words better, given that the cafeteria had gotten louder by now. The woman crossed her legs and mimicked Billy's motion.

    "I know a bar, not too far from where I live. It's called the Psychic," she explained. "It might not be your usual bar and it's not as big as most others, but the prices are acceptable and it's pretty cozy."

    Billy wrote the name of the place down in his notebook.

    "They usually don't let customers celebrate parties at their place, but I happen to know the owner pretty well." She took another sip of her coffee. "Just tell her that the Green Witch sent you. I'm sure she'll give you a free pass."

    "The Green Witch?" Agatha asked with a chuckle.

    Rio shrugged her shoulders.

    "The original one," she said with a coy smile.

    The young man's face lit up at Rio's words. After he was finished writing, he looked up at her again, gifting her a teeth glaring grin. "Thank you so much, Miss Vidal. I really appreciate it!"

    "No need to thank me."

    She leaned back in her chair, eyeing Agatha who was clapping her hands together.

    "Good, you got your location. Now you and your bestie Alice know where to head after work," she said while motioning her hands to signal Billy to hurry away.

    "Alice has a long shift today, she won't be able to make it in time," he said. "Then go tomorrow."

    "Time's running out, Agatha. I need to do it today."

    "Ask Wanda?"

    "Mom won't be home til midnight."

    "It's a bar, Billy. Most of them are open almost 24/7."

    "Please, Agatha, can you just come with me instead? It's really urgent," he asked again.

    Agatha sighed loudly and thought about an excuse. "I'm really busy right now. I still got plenty of reports to work on-"

    "I think you should go with him." Rio said, making Agatha turn to her. "Hey, whose side are you on?" Agatha mouthed, earning a teasing grin from the CEO. Rio felt herself mildly enjoying the slight annoyance in Agatha's tone.

    "I'll make sure you won't get any additional tasks for the day, and then the two of you can handle this issue together," she said and got up from her seat.

    Agatha eyed Billy again, who was smiling brighter by the second. He grabbed his notebook and got up from his seat as well.

    "Alright, fine. But only under the condition that you'll be driving me home after."

    "Of course," the boy said.

    With that, the three of them made their way out of the cafeteria. Billy kept thanking his boss for this opportunity, Rio only ever dismissing it as a simple favor. As the young man strutted back to his workplace, Rio halted in front of a conference room.

    Agatha looked after the boy with a pout, halting in her steps when she recognized the other woman not following along.

    "Use it as a chance to get your mind off for some time. I can't have you drooling over my reports again," Rio said.

    Agatha turned towards her, crossing her arms in front of her chest. Before she could further protest, Rio's hand reached out for her right arm, squeezing it tenderly.

    "Let me know if it worked out," she said before reverting into the space of the conference room. Agatha watched as she closed the door, still feeling Rio's hand linger on her upper arm. Her own hand wandered over to the spot, fingertips stroking it mindlessly.

    She made her way back to the cubicle as well. In a few hours, after finishing her daily tasks, she would go on the (involuntary) quest to find this peculiar bar, and whoever was running it.

    Later on, Agatha and Billy walked down the street that was supposed to lead them to the bar Rio had mentioned hours prior. The young man was looking at his phone, trying to decipher the description Google Maps showed him.

    "It should be right around the corner," he said.

    Agatha imagined what they could be met with. Given that she didn't even know about the tiny asian restaurant she paid a visit some time ago, she mustered that the infamous bar would also look as inconspicuous. "So, uhm, what exactly were you and Miss Vidal doing back at the cafeteria?"

    "We went over a report, talking about work."

    "With your hand on her back, staring longingly at her?"

    She looked up at him. All she was met with was a sly smile. "That's none of your business, really."

    "Since when have you two gotten so close?"

    They reached a crossroad and moved over the street, following its direction further down the line.

    "We're not close."

    "It sure looks like it. I've never seen her in the cafeteria before, especially not cuddled up to the likes of you."

    "We were not 'cuddled up', Billy. She was having a moment and I tried to console her, that is all."

    "You were consoling her? Oh, you're definitely close to her. And whipped." A scoff left Agatha's mouth. "'Whipped'? What are you, five?" "Inches taller than you? Sure am-"

    Agatha's hand met the back of Billy's head in an instant. The boy brought up his hands in a defensive gesture, grinning sheepishly. He watched Agatha as she gestured towards a building.

    "There it is, come on."

    She pulled on the sleeve of his jacket and dragged him along as if walking a pet. He followed after her with quick steps until the both of them stood before the entrance of the bar. A big neon sign adorned the top of the door, reading 'PSYCHIC'. Right besides the door, there was a big window, giving way to look inside.

    "Let's get this over with," Agatha breathed and moved to open the door. As soon as she stepped into the bar, the atmosphere shifted significantly. The air was thicker than outside, mixed with smoke, alcohol and sweat. She noticed a few people turning around in their seats to look at the two unknown faces stepping into their place.

    Billy held onto Agatha, as he was met with sinister looks of a group of men sitting at a desk playing poker. His eyes wandered through the bar, recognizing the unusual decoration adorning the walls. Various signs were placed alongside the structure, shiny crystals laying on top of shelves, as well as several trinkets and rather obscure-looking objects being sprawled across the room.

    "I really dig the vibe of this bar," he said quietly.

    When Agatha found the counter to be empty and unoccupied, she started tapping on the dark wood, scanning the area for the owner. Her eyes wandered over to the men sitting at a table nearby. Agatha mustered that most of them were already in their sixties, their white and grey hair standing out in contrast to the dark clothes they were wearing. One of them was looking over his shoulder to inspect both Agatha and Billy intensely. "Hey big guy, you the owner of this bar?" Agatha said, feeling his thousand yard stare impale her.

    "That would be me," a feminine voice appeared from afar. A woman entered the bar from a room behind the counter and revealed herself to Agatha and Billy.

    "What can I do for you?" the woman asked. She was a little older than Agatha presumed, the curly hair which was held up in a bun showing off a deep grey colour. She didn't wear anything usual for someone who owned a bar, but given the way the interior looked, the woman fit right in, blending well with the many curious trinkets. The brunette felt the woman scrutinizing her slowly.

    "Why, hello there," Agatha put on a big smile, "The both of us came here in a hurry to settle an urgent matter. We were looking for a location to celebrate a party and happened to stumble upon your fine establishment." The woman looked at Billy and then back to Agatha.

    "We don't do children's birthday parties in here. I think what you're looking for would be the McDonalds party bus," the woman said, earning a chuckle from some of the men sitting further from them.

    Billy approached the counter as well, placing his hands on the wood. "Oh no, sorry, Miss-"

    "Calderu."

    "Miss Calderu, this party is not meant for me. A friend of mine, who is turning 33, is currently searching for a location and we happened to be led to your bar."

    She shook her head.

    "Well, it doesn't matter how old anyone is turning, as I usually don't allow parties here, anyway. You must know, this is more of a private bar, and I don't think I have seen the two of you here before. So, if you don't plan on having a drink you're welcome to leave."

    Agatha felt the eyes of the men linger on her and Billy.

    "You heard Lilia, get lost!" one of them shouted.

    The bar owner pointed her hand towards the door, showing the two of them the way out. Billy tapped Agatha's arm multiple times in order to get her to speak. After realizing that she wouldn't, he took it upon himself to follow Rio's advice.

    "The Green Witch sent us," he said, loud enough for the entire room to hear. Agatha put a hand on her forehead and started rubbing it. What came out of Billy's mouth sounded utterly ridiculous, sparking a wave of embarassment within the woman. Surprisingly, none of the customers appeared to be irritated by what he had said. Instead, they started whispering to each other before resuming their play at poker.

    Lilia lowered her hand and started mustering the both of them again. Her mimic morphed into a questioning one.

    "Rio sent you?" she asked.

    "She adviced us to go and meet you," Billy explained.

    "And she's not even bothering to turn up herself. As usual", Lilia mumbled, mostly to herself.

    Agatha watched as the woman stepped behind the counter. She motioned for them to sit down, Billy doing as ordered, Agatha following a few seconds after, involuntarily at that.

    "Do you want anything?" she asked.

    "No," Agatha said.

    "We also have non alcoholic drinks."

    "I'll have a coke, then," Billy said. "Please."

    After getting him the drink, Lilia stopped in front of Agatha and started cleaning a set of dirty glasses. The brunette grew impatient. Agatha wished to get this concern over with and not meddle with useless smalltalk. "So, to whom do I owe the pleasure, exactly?" Lilia asked. "I'm Billy Maximoff, and this is-"

    "Agatha Harkness," Lilia finished the sentence, her eyes remaining glued on the brunette. Agatha had mustered that the woman must've recognized her as soon as she laid eyes on her. "What are the likes of you doing here?" "That's not important right now," Agatha said, not wanting to recite her life's story in the midst of a lousy bar. "About the party, is it managable or not?"

    "Depends." Lilia set down a glass, grabbing the next one shortly after. "When do you plan on celebrating?"

    "Next Saturday," Billy shot in and took a sip of the soda. "Next Saturday!?"

    "Well, I did mention it was urgent," Agatha mumbled.

    "The location we had ordered previously canceled on us today. That's why you're our only hope to fix this, Miss Calderu," Billy explained. The last freshly cleaned glass left Lilia's hands and disappeared right behind the counter. The woman propped herself up on the wood.

    "That's going to be tough, but given that you're friends of Rio, I think I'll manage."

    Billy smiled triumphantly. "Yes, thank you so much!"

    "How is she doing?" Lilia asked an prepared a bowl of crisps for her new guests. She placed it on top of the counter, watching as Billy's hand instinctively moved towards it. "Help yourself, kid."

    "She's doing fine. Lots and lots of work, but overall, fine," Agatha said. "Where do you know her from?" Lilia asked curiously.

    Billy gulfed down a handful of crisps before answering the question. "She's our boss."

    Lilia looked at Agatha in disbelief.

    "You're working for Vidal?"

    Agatha's eyebrows scrunched in confusion. Lilia recognized who she was almost instantly, but didn't know about her working at her family's rival company? The entire city must've heard about it by now.

    "Never thought I'd see that happen," Lilia said.

    "Neither did I," Agatha uttered.

    "And she doesn't even bother telling me that, either", Lilia said to herself, her eyes showing a rather sad expression.

    Rio had mentioned that they knew each other pretty well, but it was still a mystery to Agatha how exactly both of them were related. Before they entered the bar, Agatha thought about the possibility of the owner being one of Rio's relatives, or exes, but this seemed to be something completely different. Multiple questions were burning the tip of Agatha's tongue. "So, about your party. How many people can I expect?" Lilia resumed the topic.

    Billy took out his phone. He had a list written down with all the people that would attend the party. It did look a little scarce if he was honest. "Uhm, first off, Jen will of course be attending. Alice, me, Corinna and Ben ... oh, and Johnny. Which makes us six."

    Lilia raised an eyebrow and looked at Agatha.

    "You're not even part of it?"

    "Birthday kid's not the biggest fan of me."

    The bar owner grabbed a piece of paper that was lying nearby, writing the information down quickly. Agatha watched her movements, recognizing the many rings Lilia was wearing on her fingers.

    "At what time?"

    "Is 8pm alright?" Billy asked.

    "Six people. 8pm. Ordered by Maximoff. Got it."

    Agatha watched Billy empty his soda.

    "Okay, now that we got everything, I think it's time to head back home," she chimed enthusiastically Before she could get up from her seat, Lilia raised a hand to urge the brunette to wait. She dithered on the spot, burying her hands between her thighs and letting her shoulders sink lower. "I'll only make an exception for you under two conditions." Lilia stretched out her forefinger and middlefinger. Billy nodded, awaiting what the woman wanted to ask for.

    "First, you'll assure me that Rio turns up next Saturday, too. She hasn't shown herself in ages, if she wants me to do a favor for her, she should at least come around and say hello."

    Agatha looked at Billy, who already looked back at her.

    "I mean, Jen didn't mention inviting her, but she doesn't really mind her either. I guess you could ask her to come. You're closer to her than I am," he said.

    Agatha sighed.

    "Fine, I'll ask her."

    Lilia smiled contently and lowered one finger.

    "Second, I don't do all of this for free. Organizing a party needs effort and stocked up alcohol. I take payment in advance."

    Billy gave Agatha an awkward smile.

    "I ... don't have any money on me right now."

    "God damn it," the brunette scoffed and then turned back to Lilia, "... do you take credit cards?"

    Lilia pointed a payment device at Agatha, who had already grabbed her card while angrily staring at Billy. He was mouthing a series of silent sorrys to her.

    "How much!?" Agatha exclaimed.

    "It's the fairest price you'll get around here. The coke's already included," Lilia explained.

    Agatha typed in her number.

    "Tips are also greatly appreciated."

    The brunette's chest heaved greatly. Her hand moved back into her purse, grabbing a five dollar bill and laying it onto Lilia's already outstretched hand.

    "Thank you, and see you next week at the Psychic," the woman said. Billy and Agatha got up from their seats and walked past the other guests of the establishment. As soon as they hit the fresh air outside, Agatha started strutting down the direction they've walked before.

    "Thank you, Agatha. You saved my ass."

    "Tell me something new."

    They walked to the car in a quick pace. Billy pulled the sleeves of his jacket over his fingers and looked down at Agatha.

    "Will you come as well?"

    "You read it yourself. I am not on the list so, no, I won't come." "Well, you said you'd ask Miss Vidal to come."

    "And what has that got to do with me?"

    "Wouldn't you want to accompany her?"

    Agatha halted in her steps.

    "You're really getting the wrong idea here, Billy. We're not close, we're not friends. I'll ask her to come simply to meet Lilia's conditions, nothing more and nothing less."

    Billy nudged his head to the side. He looked at the woman in front of her, who was actively avoiding eye contact.

    "You don't like her?", he asked softly.

    "Oh come on, she makes it hard not to like her."

    "I do have a feeling she really likes you, too."

    Agatha's eyes beamed up at him. She decided to get back to walking again, Billy following close in her steps.

    "She's nice to everyone, if you haven't noticed already. Besides, I don't even know if she's into women, no reason to get anyone's hopes up." "So you are interested in her."

    "I did not say that. I said if anyone were to get their hopes up, they would probably end up disappointed. Clean your ears."

    "Man, you sure lie a lot but you're really bad at making this one sound convincing."

    When they finally reached Billy's car, Agatha took a hold of the door handle. As she proceeded to open the door, she realized the car was still locked.

    "By the way, I do have a feeling that Miss Vidal is not straight. I know, one should never assume, but my gaydar peaked the moment I met her for the first time."

    "Your what?"

    "Gaydar. It's, like, an additional sense queer people have, which makes it easy to identify fellow other queer people. You should have it as well, you know."

    Agatha only blinked in confusion.

    "Hold on, don't tell me you've never felt it before? It's like a vibe you get f-" "Stop yapping and open the fucking car!"

    He did as he was told, the both of them sitting in the vehicle shortly after. Billy took off to drive Agatha back home. The latter looked out of the window, watching as they passed street after street.

    "You know, if you really are interested in her, maybe I could try and push you two in a direction."

    Agatha let out a hearty chuckle.

    "I'd rather jump out of this car right now than have you as my wingman." "Ouch. I'm not that bad, I swear."

    The brunette felt her smartphone vibrate and was met with Rio's name. What a coincidence.

    "Oh my god, you have Miss Vidal's number!?" Billy exclaimed, his eyes growing wide with curiosity.

    "Keep your eyes on the road and stop being nosey."

    "What did she text you?"

    "None of your business."

    Agatha put her phone away without reading the message, earning a scoff from Billy in return. He looked straight forward again, his concentration returning to driving.

    "You're definitely whipped."

    "Shut up"

    The road they were taking finally ended up at Agatha's apartment complex. She grabbed her belongings and wrapped her hand around the handle of the car's door.

    "Would you mind coming up for a second? I got something for you," she asked, receiving a nod from Billy who got out of the vehicle right after her. They went up several stairs, passed a few other apartments and then ended up at Agatha's home. She opened the door quickly and the both of them entered the flat one after the other. Billy followed Agatha to the living room and watched as she put her bag on the desk.

    "Wait a second," she said before disappearing in her bedroom. About a minute after, she came back with a shirt in her hands.

    "Ask your mother to wash this, I can't get those stains out for the life of me. She's an expert when it comes to red, I'm sure she'll know what to do." She handed him the shirt. He figured it to be the one Agatha was wearing the day she got tomato soup all over her. Billy accepted it with slight disappointment.

    "And here I thought you'd get me a present or something." "You owe me 68 bucks. I ain't getting you nothing."

    Billy sighed and made his way back to the entrance. Agatha followed and opened the door for him.

    "Thank you for dropping me off, Billy. Text me when you get home." The young man nodded and walked through the door.

    "Sure will. Good night, Agatha."

    "Night", she said with a smile.

    Once the door was closed, Agatha followed her usual night routine. After getting out of the shower, she finally took a look at the message Rio had sent her some time ago.

    Rio: How did it go?

    Agatha: we managed to get the location The brunette started brushing her hair, wet locks gaining a less chaotic shape stroke by stroke. She heard her phone jingle again. Rio for sure was quick at texting back.

    Rio: glad to hear that you got it sorted out Agatha: there's one thing, though Lilia only allowed Billy to plan this if you turn up as well Rio: I figured she'd want something like that Agatha finished brushing her hair and hovered her fingers over the phone screen. She started formulating a question about Lilia, but before she could finish it, Rio had already texted her again.

    Rio: Will you be there as well?

    Agatha: nope, not invited Rio: neither am I exactly Agatha: Lilia wants you there Rio: okay, if that's what it takes what if I want you there, then?

    Electricity ran through Agatha's veins in an instant. She felt her heartbeat speed up. This couldn't possibly be happening right now, could it? Her own boss, being playful all of a sudden? Certainly not on Agatha's bingo card. The employee in her screamed to answer in a fashionable manner, but the woman in her was all but fussing about every possible answer she could formulate with only 26 available letters.

    Agatha: if my own fangirl says so, I might as well consider it The name felt risky, but Agatha was not afraid to dip into a little dangerous territory.

    Rio: don't think too long about it, my idol I hate waiting A satisfied feeling came over her at the sight of Rio's answer. Who would've thought that she, of all people, would get to call her boss by a pet name and get one for herself in return? Agatha thought it to be slightly ridiculous at first, but the more times she repeated reading Rio's text, she came to like the idea even more that her own boss looked up at her. It made her feel validated in a way she hasn't felt in a long time. Not that she'd ever openly admit that to anyone, of course.

    Agatha: noted She slipped under her covers, smartphone still in her hands. It vibrated again, showing off Billy's name on the top.

    Billy: got home safely Agatha: good Billy: you asked Ms Vidal yet?

    Agatha: I did. Sort of.

    Billy: what did she say??

    Agatha: she said she's going to lay you off as soon as possible because you're annoying as hell Agatha was quickly met with the GIF of a crying hamster.

    Agatha: I'm pretty sure she'll come consider your party safe, you can thank me by sending 80 bucks my way Billy: thx so much!!

    I will try to give you the money back asap Agatha: you better Billy: wait, you said it was only 68 Agatha: tax included. it's an unfair world, get used to it When no additional message appeared in the chat, Agatha decided to get back to Rio's line of messages. A new text popped up at the bottom. Rio: by the way, I let Mr. Proctor know about your progress. You should get in touch with him the following days Agatha: that's great, thank you Rio: no worries, just doing the usual I gotta head to bed now, early meeting tomorrow Agatha: ugh, those are the worst Rio: say it louder for the people in the back Agatha's fingers started writing another message. This would be the second time she'd wish her boss a good night via text, and oddly enough, it felt a little less weird than the last time. If Wanda could read her texts right now, she wouldn't stop teasing Agatha about how cheesy all of it sounded. At this point, even Agatha thought it was hard to fend off her accusations. They were of course not true, whatsoever.

    Agatha: rest well, Rio.

    Rio: sweet dreams, Agatha.

    It felt even more personal this time, too. Rio was more open about the way she worded her thoughts via text than she was in person. Agatha thought about how little she must've actually known her, now that those texts seem to gradually reveal Rio's facade behind the mask of a CEO. She was eager to find out more about the unknown.

    Maybe she would have to attend the party as well, after all. Chapter End Notes Lilia Calderu joins the coven!

    Chapter 6: Chapter Six

    Chapter Summary Agatha has her first meeting at Vidal Corp. and manages to secure a win.

    Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Agatha's foot was tapping the floor impatiently. Her arms were wrapped around her belongings, clinging close to the reports Rio had asked her to work on. When the colour of the traffic light turned green, dozens of people strutted to the other side, most of them on their way to work in the early morning. The brunette followed along, almost turning invisible between the many tired faces crossing the street.

    Her mind moved straight forward to the upcoming meeting. She wasn't nervous by all means. This was something she had done hundreds of times before, but she did wonder how working together with Proctor would turn out eventually. The both of them had consulted each other at the beginning of the week, talking about the proposals they prepared in concern of Rio's reports. Today would be the day they'd present their accumulated results in one of the company's regular finance meetings.

    A few steps from the entrance of the company, Agatha heard Billy call out her name.

    "Agatha, wait!"

    The brunette made sure to fasten in her steps.

    "Agatha!" she heard an additional voice call her.

    She turned around on her heels, meeting the faces of not one, but two Maximoffs.

    "What are you doing here?" Agatha asked Wanda with a puzzled expression.

    "Dropping Billy off," she explained.

    "Mh, lucky guy," the brunette uttered, giving him a jealous expression. At least he didn't have to commute in an overfilled train, stuffed to the brim with way too many sweaty people.

    "I gotta head to office now. Bye, mom."

    He gave Wanda a smile, then turned to nod at Agatha.

    "See ya, Agatha."

    Agatha didn't even bother answering him, instead returning her focus on the redhead in front of her.

    Billy walked towards the building in a rush when he almost crashed right into his boss. Rio halted right in front of him, managing to stop an accident just in time.

    "Oh, sorry, Miss Vidal," he apologized.

    "All good;" she said, giving him a smile. She grabbed the door handle and offered entrance to her employee. He ducked his head in thanks, strutting right into the building.

    "Thank you," he mumbled before disappearing in the entrance hall. Rio was about to follow him in his tracks, when she registered Agatha's voice from not too far away. She turned around to meet the sight of her taking the hold of a shirt from a woman, who Rio could've sworn is someone she had met before.

    "Took you long enough to finally give it back to me," her employee said to the redhead.

    "Hey, it took me a while to get the stains out. Next time I'll just keep it and wear it myself," the other said as the cloth left her hands. Agatha grabbed it and pushed it into her bag with hurried motions.

    Rio stopped holding the door and decided to not enter the building just yet. Instead, she took a hold of her phone and warped trough a series of apps, all the while listening to the women in secret. She knew damn well that it wasn't professional in the slightest to simply linger and listen to someone else's conversation. Maybe she should've followed Billy inside, but the urge of finding out what that shirt meant was stronger than maintaining professionalism.

    In addition to the shirt, the thought of Billy struck Rio with an old question. It wasn't too long ago that she almost asked Agatha about Billy when they had talked about Agatha's accounting method. She had mentioned something about being tired of playing his babysitter, insinuating that she has known him for years already. He just so happened to arrive to the business complex together with Agatha and this mysterious woman. Billy's interview.

    Rio suddenly remembered the redhead's identity. She was the one who accompanied the young man to Rio's office and introduced herself as Wanda Maximoff, Billy Maximoff's mother.

    Rio was aware that Agatha had known Billy ever since he was a child, which could only mean that she also knew Wanda for at least equally as long. The older brunette hadn't mentioned anything about living with someone else, but that didn't extinguish the possibility of her seeing someone. The exchange of that shirt certainly suggested so. The way Agatha hurridly shoved it into her bag, almost as if not to draw anyone's attention to it, felt unpleasantly suspicious to Rio.

    "Billy told me you had a meeting today. Why does he know way before I do?" Wanda asked.

    "That's probably because he literally works with me, Wanda." "You could have called or at least texted me. It's progress, after all." "It's a simple finance meeting. Nothing worth mentioning." "Still would be nice to hear from you every now and then, Agatha," the redhead said.

    "Missing me that much already?"

    Wanda sighed. "Good luck at your meeting. Not that you'd need it." She took half a step back, looking over Agatha's shoulder and catching a glimpse of Rio, who immediately reverted back to staring at her phone as soon as she made eye contact with her.

    "I'll see you around," Wanda chimed and patted Agatha's arm lightly before retreating to the parking lot close by.

    Agatha quickly waved her goodbye before turning around to finally enter the building. She recognized Rio standing besides the door, gaze directed to the screen of her phone. The accountant started wondering how long her boss had been standing there already.

    "Morning, Rio," she greeted, grabbing the CEO's attention in an instant. "Morning," Rio answered in return, her lips curving up slightly. She put her phone away, gifting her focus to the woman in front of her. The accountant held the door for her boss, who was reacting with an appreciative nod. Agatha walked besides her until they reached the elevator. Once they were inside, Rio looked over to her and scanned her from head to toe. The itch to ask about Wanda and Billy grew stronger by the minute. "Excited for today's meeting?" was the question that left her mouth instead. "Not really. I don't really feel anything, if I'm honest." "Maybe it'll catch up to you once it's over," Rio said.

    "Maybe," Agatha mumbled quietly. The meeting was still a few hours away, there might be a chance for her to change her mind as the day progresses. Agatha gyrated in order to meet Rio's gaze, but the latter turned away as if being caught in an action. She felt that something was off, but wasn't exactly sure where to put her assumption. Instead of asking, she stared blankly at the elevator doors and listened to the hum of the machinery pulling them upwards.

    The doors opened shortly after, Rio gesturing Agatha to step out first. Once they were joined in the hallway together, they made their way past several rooms to get to their desired destination.

    "See you later," Rio said, gifting Agatha a short smile before retreating to her office. The accountant watched as she left, raising an eyebrow questioningly. Rio's smile was different today, as it did not quite reach her eyes.

    Agatha made her way to her cubicle, setting the many reports down on her desk. Her bag followed a moment later, showing glimpses of the shirt peaking through in between her other work utensils. Her fingers wrangled themselves around the material, taking it outside of the bag and putting it in one of the drawers of her desk. If there ever happened to be another soup incident again, Agatha knew to be prepared.

    She slumped down on her chair and started working on her daily tasks.

    Several hours after lunch break, Agatha met up with Mr. Proctor in order to prepare for the upcoming meeting. They went over their reports and proposals, assuring that they could work in tandem to successfully present their efforts. The thought of sharing a stage with someone else was jarring, but Agatha decided it would be best to simply go with it. Soon enough, she'd get to attend meetings on her own accord again, without any additional burden.

    They made their way to the conference room, finding the people they'd be presenting to already occupying their seats. After shaking each of their hand and greeting them, Agatha caught a glimpse of Rio entering the room. "Sorry for the wait. My other meeting lasted a little longer than originally planned," the CEO said, her eyes scanning the many faces who joined them in this meeting. She greeted every member of the audience individually before sitting down. She actively avoided eye contact with Agatha, another thing that stood out to the accountant. Something was off. "Alright, now that everyone is here, we can proceed with the presentation," one of the men declared. He gestured towards Mr. Proctor and Agatha, giving them the signal to start.

    "Mr. Proctor, Ms. Harkness, please, the stage's yours."

    Proctor immediately started out with his portion of the presentation, talking about the culminated information about the current financial status. Agatha eyed him with attention, waiting for her part to come.

Investments have accelerated general profits by almost 2 percent in such a short amount of time," Proctor reported.     "Agatha Harkness," the person at the other end of the line said, voice more stern this time.

    "What?!"

    "There's something we have to talk about."

    "Oh, you want to talk?" Agatha said in a mocking tone. She felt her breath getting shaky. "It's a little too late for that, don't you think?" "It's-"

    "Your messages have told me enough. I don't need you to repeat yourself over and over-"

    "I have an offer for you, Agatha."

    Her mind blanked for a second.

    "What? Need me to pull your plug? Cause if that's the case I'd happily oblige."

    "I'ts about the business," Evanora continued. "I am sure you'd be interested in what I have to say."

    Agatha halted. She couldn't believe what she was hearing. All this time after her layoff, her mother hadn't shown any interest in letting her in on anything regarding the business.

    "What do you mean?" Agatha asked. Her voice decreased in volume. "It would be best if we discussed that in private. Come and meet me in my office, and then I will tell you everything you need to know." Agatha grabbed her phone tighter. Her brain was actively searching for her next words, unsure of how to react to her mother's sudden offer. This sounded a little too unreal to begin with.

    "Tell me now," was the only thing she could spurt out. "Now, or else I won't-"

    "I will be in my office until nine. Show up in time or else I won't tell you about anything at all."
