    With an audible sigh, she put her phone back in her pocket. She remained in place for a few seconds before resuming her road towards the trainstation.

    "I can't believe I'm doing this," Agatha uttered to herself. She hopped off the train way earlier than normal, making her way towards the very place she swore herself to never return to.

    "Agatha, what are you-," the receptionist asked, but ultimately got cut off. "Shut it, Greg. Have mother dearest know her lovely daughter has come to visit her," she snarled.

    "Uh, sure, right on it."

    Agatha tapped on the desk impatiently, watching as the man called up to Evanora's office. Greg eyed her the entire time he waited for the woman to pick up.

    "Come on, my grandmother could manage this quicker than you," Agatha scoffed. "All that's left of her is dirt and she'd still do a better job." Greg put the phone away and gestured towards the elevator. "Mrs. Harkness is ready to welcome you in about ten minutes." "What a delight," Agatha huffed before disappearing in the designated direction.

    She swayed past many unfamiliar faces, not bothering to greet any of them. Once she ended up at her mother's office, she remained almost frozen in place. Her hand was close to opening the door, one simple push and she'd be at her destination.

    After a long, deep breath, Agatha flung the door open without announcing herself beforehand.

    Startling the man that was currently sitting opposite Evanora, Agatha strutted straight towards the desk.

    "You wanted me? Here I am."

    "Excuse you, we are in the middle of a meeting," the man exclaimed. Agatha ignored his existence and stepped further towards Evanora. Said woman tried to calm her visitor down, clearly upset about the interruption. "Agatha, how dare you burst into my office like this? Wait another five minutes and I'll be ready for you."

    "If I remember correctly, it was you who called me here. I won't wait another five minutes until you're done with whatever scam you try to conjure upon this fella," Agatha snarled. "I am here now, spit it out. What do you want?"

    Evanora excused her guest, the man making his way out of the office shortly after.

    "Reconsider selling your soul to this hag," Agatha shot towards him. "No one has ever gotten it back."

    "Agatha," Evanora exclaimed, finger pointing at the seat in front of the desk, now unoccupied. "Sit down."

    The brunette moved closer to the desk, not bothering to take the offered seat but instead keeping her distance. She crossed her arms in front of her chest, awaiting whatever Evanora was so pressed to tell her about. "Sit down, Agatha."

    "Spare me the formalities, mother. I can hear you perfectly fine." Evanora shifted in her seat. Her expression was as stern as ever, showing no changes in what Agatha was used to. Still, Agatha couldn't help but notice that the energy changed, plastered with something oddly unfamiliar. Her gaze drifted through the scenery before her, scanning for any signs of visible change. All that stood out to her was a new, but already decaying plant in Evanora's old shelf and a bright, empty sticky note close to the unoccupied chair. The man must've lost it when he stomped out of the office before.

    "I believe we can cut it short, then," the older woman said, eyes fixating on her daughter.

    Fury now intermingled with impatience, sending Agatha into a whirlwind of emotions. It's been over a year since she last stepped foot into this office, the exact moment of it still weighing heavy on her heart. "You can get back into business."

    Without any warning, Agatha's mind went blank. Of all the things she expected to hear from her mother, this was the last thing she'd have ever considered. After everything her mother had thrown at her, this wasn't even a consideration at all.

    She thought about all possible answers she could drop at her mother's feet now, but instead, all that escaped Agatha's lips was a loud, lengthy fit of laughter.

    "This is not a joke, Agatha."

    "Is it not? Because I actually find that one pretty amusing," Agatha said. "Do you honestly believe that after everything that happened, I'd ever consider the idea of working for you again?"

    Evanora grabbed a hold of a few papers, watching intently as Agatha shook her head several times. Her expression got overwhelmed with anger immediately after letting out the last chuckle.

    "After everything you have done to me? After everything you have taken from me?"

    Agatha clenched her jaw.

    "Even if you were on your knees begging for me to come back, I'd still refuse."

    Evanora only raised an eyebrow in reaction.

    "Is that not what you wanted, Agatha?" she asked. "To lead this company?" "Oh miracles, you finally managed to clock that as well," Agatha exclaimed and clapped her hands together. "Took you long enough to realize that. It's a little too late for it though, now that you declined that possibility from me and kicked me out of the entire business instead."

    Agatha took a deep breath before shaking her head another time. "Shove that offer where the sun doesn't shine," she hissed. "I won't ever work for you again."

    With one last condescending look, she turned on her heels and started strutting back towards the exit.

    "I did not mention anything about working for me," Evanora said. "You'd get into the position you were designated for and therefore won't be working under my guidance."

    Agatha's hand hovered over the handle of the door. Hesitancy claimed her, keeping her from leaving the office.

    Evanora's gaze bore right into her daughter's still form. Agatha could feel it tear itself right through her ribs.

    "You can get back into business and claim your position as CEO," Evanora continued slowly.

    Agatha tried to break out of her trance. She turned slightly, handle still in reach. One swift motion and she could open that very door and leave. Leave her mother behind, leave that company behind, the way she had done a year ago when she stood in that exact same place.

    "That's not true," Agatha uttered. "You're lying."

    "I am not."

    Agatha looked over her shoulder, only to be met with a thick document, tightly grasped in the older woman's hand. Evanora let it slump onto the desk with a quiet thud.

    "It's already written down on paper," Evanora explained. She tapped on the documents with a pen. Agatha listened to the motions, the noise seemingly growing louder with each tap.

    Everything inside of her screamed. One part of her was pulling herself back towards Evanora's desk, the other weakly tugged her further out of the office. Everything was too loud, too chaotic, too much.

    Her hand remained close to the handle. Agatha watched her fingers starting to shake.

    "This is a contract which contains every detail about how you will get back into Harkness Corp," Evanora's voice returned.

    After taking in a staggered breath, Agatha retracted her hand. She let her gaze carefully roam through the office before making her way towards Evanora.

    Her eyes dropped low, avoiding Evanora and instead, focusing her attention on the document ahead. She pushed herself further down the line, crossing the distance she held previously and ending up right at the edge of her mother's desk.

    The sticky note that still remained on the floor caught her attention again. Agatha decided to carefully hide it under her right foot and draw it close to her.

    "You're lying," Agatha repeated.

    "See for yourself. All of it has been legally documented already." Her hands moved towards the contract. Shaking fingers roamed the sides of the papers, turning the pages one after the other.

    "You will get back into this business as the subsitute CEO and remain in said position until I leave mine. The date of my withdrawal is set in this contract and will be the exact day you will get elevated to head of this company."

    Agatha let her eyes wander over the document. The date of withdrawal was near and already adorned with Evanora's signature.

    The brunette let out a small chuckle. It was faint, barely recognizable. "You'd never willingly give up this company yourself and withdraw," Agatha mumbled.

    "I am getting old, Agatha. I cannot lead this company forever." "You'd take it to your grave if you could."

    Agatha let one finger roam over the text that adorned the contract. The paper felt warm.

    "You're only doing this because business is failing."

    Agatha's eyes met those of her mother, staring coldly.

    "Everyone in this city knows that your beloved business is going down the drain. Revenues are sinking, people are leaving their positions, all that besides multiple claims of fraud ..." Agatha uttered. "The company you spent your entire life building is slowly, but surely, fading to dust and you can't face the fact that you're incapable of changing its fate." She laid her hands plainly on the document.

    "You're backing away because you're facing your biggest failure yet," Agatha continued. "And the only way of accepting that is through bestowing it upon your first failure, am I right?"

    Evanora leaned further against the desk. She tapped the tip of her pen onto the contract again.

    "You can either convince yourself of the things that could be, or, you could take the contract in consideration and realize that my withdrawal is already systematically arranged and has nothing to do with the current situation of the company," Evanora said. "Harkness Corp. is still doing great, and will continue to do so with the right lead. The contract's contents are set in stone and only require your signature to fully roll into motion." "Whatever happened to me not deserving another chance?" Agatha dared to ask.

    Evanora let out a long, deep sigh.

    "I have ... changed my mind," she explained slowly. "The decision of letting you go may have been a bit undercalculated."

    "Undercalculated?" Agatha felt her muscles tense. "You call what you did an undercalculated action?"

    "That's because it was. I may have taken it a step too far, without considering the future ahead," Evanora said. She paused for some time, all the while fixating her gaze on Agatha.

    "And for that," Evanora continued. "I am sorry."

    Agatha's head felt heavy. Never, in her entire life, has she ever heard her own mother apologize to her. Those words had the shape of a forbidden artifact; something that would never cross Evanora's vocabulary in presence of her daughter.

    Her throat felt tight. Her eyes started to burn. This was all Agatha had ever wanted to hear from her, but even now, amidst the realisation that she indeed just witnessed Evanora letting those words slip past, she knew deep in her heart that they had neither value nor depth.

    Those words meant nothing to her. But Agatha so deeply wanted them to. Evanora kept eyeing her. The sensation of being watched kept pressing into her, eloping another wave of uneasiness within Agatha.

    In order to press forward with the matter and to evade further delusions, Agatha cleared her throat and tried to focus her mind on the task ahead. "Who did you construct this contract with? Kingo?" Agatha asked, gulping down the sting that came with said name.

    "The creator is of no importance. It was constructed with legal consultation and stands as it is."

    Agatha's eyes roamed over the document again.

    A contract has gotten her out of the business before. Another is indeed capable of reversing her situation. Even though the company was most likely doing as bad as publicists conclude, Agatha was sure that her absence was only a factor that fed into it. She was sure that if anyone were able to turn the tides and get it going again, it'd be her.

    "An offer from you never comes without a price," Agatha said. "What do you want in return?"

    "Merely one simple favor."

    "And that would be?"

    The brunette watched as her mother leaned back in her seat. Evanora's expression remained stern, but the corner of her lips moved up into a weak grin.

    "You're currently working for Harkness Corp's biggest rival," Evanora huffed.

    Agatha unknowingly pushed herself a little farther from the desk. "Vidal has always been a thorn in my side and will continue to be so for the company."

    "And you want me to do what, exactly?"

    "According to my knowledge, you happen to be very close to their source," Evanora continued. "Something unrecognizable would already suffice. A small mistake in a certain department that could grow into greater chaos, affecting the company as a whole would be enough to make ends meet." Agatha shook her head in disbelief.

    "You want me to manipulate them? How?"

    Evanora nudged her head to the side.

    "I am more than convinced that the likes of you are able to figure something out."

    "You do know that's illegal and considered corporate espionage. That's a risk not worth taking."

    "Do not petend like you have always handled business according to lawful guidelines, Agatha."

    The brunette let her eyes wander from the contract to Evanora, before fixating her gaze on the papers again. She hated how much they called out to her.

    This could turn the tides completely. Agatha would get back to where she was meant to be, and all it would take is one simple scheme she was more than capable of. Everything would get back to what it was. What it was supposed to be from the start.

    It was only logical to at least take it into consideration. "Will you accept this offer, or not?"

    Agatha's eyes met Evanora's again. Her throat suddenly felt dry. "I ..."

    Another look at the document had her feel an invisible string tying itself around her neck. Her mind was reeling with answers, screaming yes all over. A yes would seal it all; a verbal contract. It'd be enough to cage Agatha in, leaving no room for escape.

    "I need more time," Agatha managed to choke out.

    "This is an urgent matter that requires an answer," Evanora said in a stern voice. "Yes or no, Agatha?"

    When Agatha did not answer, Evanora started shifting in her seat vigorously and taking a hold of the contract. Agatha watched carefully as her mother's attention averted from her, using the opportunity to subtly slide the sticky note under the desk until only a corner of it was peeking out. The older woman suddenly rose from her seat, slightly towering over the woman in front of her. Her gaze returned to Agatha in a rather condescending way, piercing itself through the frozen body that was still inspecting the contract.

    "I need more time, mother," Agatha repeated, body seemingly shrinking the more her mother's rose. In an attempt to gain more freedom from the string that was now creeping from around her neck towards her ribcage, she let out another rattled breath.

    There was a long, strenuous pause. The office felt way colder than before. Agatha felt herself starting to shiver.

    "Agatha!"

    The brunette refused to look up at her.

    "You're legally obligated to at least let me have a proper look," she whispered in a shaky voice. "I just ... need more time to go through this contract. I can't sign it blindly."

    Agatha flinched when Evanora's hand moved. She watched as it roughly shoved the contract towards Agatha, who instantly took a hold of it. "I will give you no more than a month. This is the only chance you will get from me and I expect you to own up to your side of the offer." Agatha did not give Evanora another answer. Instead, she remained quiet and did not move an inch from the desk.

    "Once everything is settled, we can arrange the formalities. In the meantime, you will figure out a way to handle Vidal's part of the deal adequately and keep updating me about it. Do you understand?" Yet again, Agatha remained quiet.

    "Do you understand, Agatha?" Evanora repeated.

    Agatha took a deep breath.

    "Yes, mother," she answered eventually.

    Her mother's expression lit up with satisfaction.

    Agatha did not hesitate to linger in her position any longer. She turned on her heels to walk back to the exit of the room.

    "I expect you to not disappoint me again."

    Agatha's hand clutched the contract tighter. Doubts were still dominating her mind, but in order to be able to form her next step, she would have to spend more time dissecting the contract down to its core. One month was still enough time to figure out a proper solution.

    A piece of paper and a couple signatures already had the power of destroying her life. Agatha was more than sure that this time, there would be a way to use it to her advantage.

    This must be the choice Lilia had told her about. Something that would change the entire trajectory of her life. She'd be damned if she didn't act logically.

    Head first, heart second.

    "I won't," Agatha whispered as she finally stepped out of the office. I don't mind."

Agatha chimed loudly. "Toodles!" "Glad I got to hear from you again, old friend," Kingo shouted after her, stretching out the last part especially. The last thing he saw was Agatha flipping him off.

    Agatha had cursed public transport enough already. This time, the sheer uncalculated amount of paperwork she had accumulated within her bag was the one to fall victim.     Agatha: ever been to Volaire's?

It's quite expensive Rio: cute Agatha clenched her fingers around the fabric of her blanket. She tried to ignore the way her heart jumped at that single, insignificant word. Rio: you do know that I could buy that place if I wanted to, right? She did know. In fact, that's what made this entire deal the more alluring. Agatha: good night, Rio The room went dark. Agatha was left to herself again, reminiscing about the events of another long day. Thought after thought, brick after brick. She grasped an opportunity; forged a plan. When given something, she'll take it and emerge with something better.

    And so she drifted off to sleep, knowing that she might have found the perfect solution to her problem.

    "Yes."

Her wild locks moving along like the gentlest of tidal waves. The skin on her collarbones tainted with the softest shade of red and painted with a galaxy of tiny freckles. Each little detail had the younger woman in complete awe. "They also got really good ricotta gnocchi. They're to die for." Rio tried to concentrate on the menu.

She didn't know that yapping about plants could be so fucking attractive.

    Rio cocked an eyebrow.

    "Like an affair?"

    Agatha's eyes beamed with arousal. Rio wasn't only pushing the boundaries, she was willing to tip-toe right along the edge. Blindfolded. "No. More like ..."

    Her eyes shot up, meeting those of her boss.

    "Simple work and play."

    "Work and play," Rio repeated breathlessly, leaning further into Agatha's touch.

    "You got it, honey. You're still my boss, and I'm still your employee. We'll carry on as usual and see where this road leads us to."

    Agatha's fingers brushed over Rio's sternum. Her skin was hot to her touch and sent a pleasant spark of electricity down the older woman's spine. "Is that enough to answer your concern?"

    "It is. Far more so than I anticipated."

    "And you're okay with it?"

    "Yes," Rio exhaled.

    "Good. So am I."

    Her hand left the woman's delicate skin and retreated back to its owner. "I'll see you at work then, Rio. Have a good night."

    Rio almost stumbled right into Agatha's flat, hadn't she grabbed onto the doorframe in time.

    "Goodnight, Agatha," she replied shakily. "Te veo."

    They watched each other leave towards their designated directions until they couldn't register the other anymore. Rio stepped down the stairs as well as her wobbly legs allowed her to, whereas Agatha fell flat against the now closed door inside the flat, wine bottle still in hand.

    Several minutes passed. She stared at the wall opposite of her and replayed the entire night in her head. Her focus shifted from Volaire's, to the wine, to the food, to Rio.

    And there it remained.

    Agatha's hand that had previously brushed along Rio's fine skin traveled up to her throat. It roamed its length, surpassed her chin and ultimately ended up reaching the softness of her bottom lip. She could smell her still. Rio had worn the same perfume she'd wear to work every single day. Agatha recognized it the moment she opened the door to welcome her. It was a good perfume, especially now that its faint aroma lingered on her very own fingers.

    The focus on the wall prevailed. Her surroundings were dimly lit, quiet and private. No awake and inconsiderate neighbors. No redhead to disturb her anymore. It was only her, an empty flat and a head full of images and scenarios alike.

    Her forefinger traced her bottom lip lazily. Agatha felt her mind getting cloudy and hazed, an effect she couldn't give the wine all its credit for. There was another feeling brooding - deeper and lower - which increased in its value and need to be released.

    Agatha contemplated for a second before reverting her attention to the wine. She wasn't going to pull through with the idea she just had. Not to the evergrowing imaginations her lust urged her to intensify, to say the least. There were other visions that could help solve the problem as well. One deep breath later and the woman heaved herself from the floor in order to stash the bottle away.

    "You're gonna have to wait a little longer. I might need you another time," she said to the wine. "And you better be fucking worth it."
